Source finding context: Order.priority / Order.is_stat / StatOrder Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:16-24 Source finding context: STAT 주문 의미가 enum 값, boolean, 하위 타입으로 세 번 표현되어 동일 개념의 권위가 갈라진다. Source finding context: STAT은 주문의 우선순위/긴급도 의미인데, 이를 값·플래그·타입으로 동시에 두면 동일 주문이 `priority=stat`, `is_stat=false`, 비-`StatOrder`처럼 모순된 상태를 가질 수 있습니다. 개념 권위 문서가 이런 중복 표현을 허용하면 EMR/LIS 간 STAT 처리, 우선순위 큐, 알림 조건의 의미가 흔들립니다. Source finding context: STAT 표현을 하나의 원천으로 통합하세요. 예를 들어 `priority` enum만 권위로 두고 `is_stat`와 `StatOrder`를 제거하거나 파생 alias로 명시합니다. `StatOrder`를 유지한다면 `priority=stat` 및 `is_stat=true`와의 불변조건을 문서화하세요. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002 Source finding context: 주문 우선순위와 STAT 주문의 EMR/LIS 공통 의미 정의 Source finding context: 통합 시스템이나 규칙이 STAT 여부를 서로 다른 필드 또는 subtype에서 읽을 때 Source finding context: 동일 STAT 개념의 해석이 갈라져 긴급 검사 라우팅, SLA/TAT 계산, 알림 정책이 서로 다른 기준으로 작동할 수 있다. Source finding context: STAT을 Order의 속성값, boolean 파생값, subtype으로 동시에 모델링했다. Source finding context: `priority` enum에 `stat` 값이 포함되어 있다. Source finding context: `is_stat`도 STAT 주문 여부라고 설명한다. Source finding context: `StatOrder` 역시 응급(STAT) 검사 주문인 `Order` 하위 타입으로 정의된다. Source finding context: 세 표현 간 동치성, 우선순위, 불일치 처리 규칙이 없다.
  - affected purpose: Shared EMR/LIS meaning for order priority and STAT orders. Source finding context: 주문 우선순위와 STAT 주문의 EMR/LIS 공통 의미 정의
  - failure condition: Integrated systems or rules read STAT status from different fields or subtype membership. Source finding context: 통합 시스템이나 규칙이 STAT 여부를 서로 다른 필드 또는 subtype에서 읽을 때
  - impact: Urgent test routing, SLA/TAT calculation, and notification policy may operate on different STAT criteria. Source finding context: 동일 STAT 개념의 해석이 갈라져 긴급 검사 라우팅, SLA/TAT 계산, 알림 정책이 서로 다른 기준으로 작동할 수 있다.
  - root hypothesis: STAT is modeled simultaneously as an Order priority value, boolean flag, and subtype without an authority or invariant rule.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:16-24`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-014`, `finding-014.cause-001`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:16`, `finding-014.cause-002`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:17`, `finding-014.cause-003`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:20-24`, `finding-014.cause-004`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-012`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.structure`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: semantics
  - action candidates: follow_up
  - materiality: The declared purpose is shared EMR/LIS meaning for order priority and STAT orders. If integrated systems read different STAT indicators, urgent routing, SLA/TAT calculations, and notification rules can diverge even for the same order, weakening predictable urgent-order handling.
  - root cause: The starting point is that one STAT concept is modeled three ways: as a priority enum value, as a boolean flag, and as a subtype. Because no authority, derivation rule, or invariant is defined among them, the model permits contradictory states instead of one shared meaning.
  - causal path: The causal path shows `priority` includes `stat`, `is_stat` also describes STAT order status, and `StatOrder` is separately defined as a STAT `Order` subtype. With no equivalence, precedence, or conflict-handling rule across those representations, an order can be interpreted as STAT by one consumer and non-STAT by another.
  - action: Choose one authoritative STAT representation, preferably the priority value if STAT is intended as order urgency, and make any retained boolean or subtype an explicit derived alias. If `StatOrder` remains, document invariants tying it to `priority=stat` and `is_stat=true`, plus how inconsistencies are rejected or resolved.

- issue-013 (medium): The `Specimen produces Result` relation is a material semantic defect because it describes specimen material as if it actively produces the lab result, instead of serving as the source material for a test, assay, or analysis event that generates the result.
  - issue stance agreement: 4/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, semantics, coverage, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: evolution, logic
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 1/1 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: semantics
  - remaining disagreement: 0/1 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: semantics
  - issue statement: Specimen produces Result misstates specimen semantics as if the specimen were the result-producing actor. Source finding context: Specimen-to-Result 관계 의미 Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:113-114 Source finding context: `Specimen produces Result`는 검체의 의미를 결과 생산 주체로 잘못 표현한다. Source finding context: `produces`는 Order가 Report를 산출하는 관계에도 쓰입니다. 같은 동사를 검체에 적용하면 검체, 검사 수행, 분석기/assay, 결과 생성의 의미 역할이 섞입니다. EMR/LIS 통합 권위 문서에서는 검체 추적과 결과 생성 주체가 구분되어야 하므로 이 관계명은 운영 해석을 흐립니다. Source finding context: `Specimen -> Result produces`를 제거하거나 `source_for`, `used_for`, `has_result`처럼 검체의 원천 역할을 드러내는 관계로 바꾸세요. 결과 생성 주체는 `Test`/`Assay` 수행 또는 분석 이벤트 개념에 연결하는 편이 의미적으로 더 정확합니다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-003 Source finding context: 검체 추적과 결과 산출 관계의 개념 권위 Source finding context: 검체-결과 lineage, 분석 수행 주체, 결과 생성 이벤트를 이 관계명에 의존해 설계할 때 Source finding context: 검체가 행위 주체처럼 모델링되어 assay/test 수행, 장비 분석, 결과 provenance의 의미 경계가 흐려진다. Source finding context: 결과의 원천인 검체에 결과 산출 행위 동사 `produces`를 부여했다. Source finding context: `Result`는 Test 수행 결과 값으로 정의된다. Source finding context: `Assay`는 분석기에서 수행되는 검사 항목으로 정의된다. Source finding context: 그런데 관계는 `Specimen -> Result produces`와 '검체가 결과를 생산한다'는 주석을 둔다. Source finding context: 같은 `produces` 관계가 `Order -> Report`에도 쓰여 산출 주체 의미를 갖는다.
  - affected purpose: Concept authority for specimen traceability and result production relationships. Source finding context: 검체 추적과 결과 산출 관계의 개념 권위
  - failure condition: Designers rely on the Specimen produces Result relation for lineage, analysis actor, or result provenance semantics. Source finding context: 검체-결과 lineage, 분석 수행 주체, 결과 생성 이벤트를 이 관계명에 의존해 설계할 때
  - impact: Specimen source, assay/test execution, analyzer action, and result provenance boundaries become ambiguous. Source finding context: 검체가 행위 주체처럼 모델링되어 assay/test 수행, 장비 분석, 결과 provenance의 의미 경계가 흐려진다.
  - root hypothesis: The model assigns the result-producing action verb to Specimen, conflating source material lineage with assay/result generation.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-003`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:49-58`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:111-114`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-015`, `finding-015.cause-001`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:57-58`, `finding-015.cause-002`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:49-55`, `finding-015.cause-003`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:113-114`, `finding-015.cause-004`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:111`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-013`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.evolution`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-016`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.structure`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: semantics
  - action candidates: follow_up
  - materiality: This weakens concept authority for specimen traceability and result-production relationships. If designers rely on this relation for lineage or provenance, the model blurs the boundary between specimen source, assay/test execution, analyzer action, and result provenance, which can lead to incorrect EMR/LIS integration interpretation.
  - root cause: The starting point is the assignment of the action verb `produces` to `Specimen`. A specimen is the material source for testing, while result production belongs to test or assay execution, or to an explicit analysis event; using an actor-like production verb on the specimen conflates those roles.
  - causal path: The causal path shows that `Result` is defined as a test execution output and `Assay` is defined as a test item performed on an analyzer, but the relation then states `Specimen -> Result produces` with a comment that the specimen produces the result. Because the same `produces` relation is also used for `Order -> Report`, where it carries an output-producing meaning, applying it to `Specimen` imports the wrong agency semantics into specimen-result lineage.
  - action: Replace or rename the relation to express specimen lineage rather than result-generation agency, using a relation such as `source_for`, `used_for`, or `has_result`. Result generation semantics should instead be connected to `Test`, `Assay`, or an explicit analysis event, so provenance and traversal paths distinguish source material from execution and production.

- issue-014 (medium): Assay is a material structural gap: it is modeled as a separate analyzer-performed execution/catalog concept, but it is not connected to ordered Tests or produced Results in the workflow graph.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, semantics, structure, logic
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 1/1 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: structure
  - remaining disagreement: 0/1 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: structure
  - issue statement: Assay is structurally orphaned from ordered tests and produced results. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml entities.Assay Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: entities.Assay defines department ref to Department and specimen_kind, but no relation includes Assay; relations list connects Order, Specimen, Test, Result, Report, Department, Staff only. Source finding context: Assay is structurally orphaned from the workflow graph. Source finding context: From the structure lens, an entity intended to represent the performed laboratory unit should be reachable from the order-to-report graph. In the current relation set, Test connects to Result, Result connects to Report and Specimen, and Department connects to Staff, while Assay is only connected through an attribute reference to Department. That leaves no structural path that says which catalog Test maps to which analyzer Assay or which Result came from an Assay. As a concept-authority document for EMR/LIS integration, this weakens the model because a key LIS-side execution concept cannot be traversed from ordered tests or produced results. Source finding context: Add explicit relations such as Test maps_to/realized_by Assay and Result produced_by Assay, or merge Assay into Test if they are not meant to be separate authority concepts yet. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-001 Source finding context: Use the ontology as the concept authority for EMR/LIS integration from Order through Report. Source finding context: When implementers need to map ordered catalog tests to LIS/analyzer execution units or trace a result back to the performed assay, the graph has no declared Test-Assay or Result-Assay connection. Source finding context: The integration contract becomes structurally incomplete for a central cross-system mapping, so different teams may create incompatible local joins outside the authority document. Source finding context: Assay was introduced as a separate execution/catalog concept without a declared structural relation to the existing order-result-report graph. Source finding context: Assay is defined as an analyzer-performed test item and separate from Test. Source finding context: The relations section does not include Assay as either a from or to endpoint. Source finding context: Therefore Assay cannot be traversed from Order, Test, Specimen, Result, or Report except indirectly sharing Department references.
  - affected purpose: Use the ontology as the concept authority for EMR/LIS integration from Order through Report.
  - failure condition: Implementers need to map ordered catalog tests to LIS/analyzer execution units or trace a result back to the performed assay. Source finding context: When implementers need to map ordered catalog tests to LIS/analyzer execution units or trace a result back to the performed assay, the graph has no declared Test-Assay or Result-Assay connection.
  - impact: The integration contract is structurally incomplete for a central cross-system mapping, pushing teams toward incompatible local joins. Source finding context: The integration contract becomes structurally incomplete for a central cross-system mapping, so different teams may create incompatible local joins outside the authority document.
  - root hypothesis: Assay was introduced as a separate execution/catalog concept without structural relations to the order-result-report graph.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-001`, `Embedded Materialized Input: entities.Assay definition and attributes`, `Embedded Materialized Input: relations list has no Assay relation`, `Embedded Materialized Input: notes says new items are registered in both Test and Assay`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-017`, `finding-017.cause-001`, `Embedded Materialized Input: entities.Assay definition`, `Embedded Materialized Input: notes: Test and Assay integration to be reconsidered; new items registered in both`, `finding-017.cause-002`, `Embedded Materialized Input: relations list`, `finding-017.cause-003`, `Embedded Materialized Input: entities.Test.department target Department`, `Embedded Materialized Input: entities.Assay.department target Department`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-014.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-014`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-014.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-014.evolution`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-013`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-015`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-014.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-014.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-014.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-006`, `rel-015`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: structure
  - action candidates: follow_up
  - materiality: This weakens the ontology's declared purpose as the concept authority for EMR/LIS integration from Order through Report because implementers cannot use the authority graph to map ordered catalog tests to LIS/analyzer execution units or trace a result back to the assay that produced it. That missing bridge leaves a central cross-system mapping outside the model and encourages incompatible local joins.
  - root cause: The starting point is that Assay was introduced as its own execution/catalog concept without structural relations to the existing order-result-report graph. Because Assay is separate from Test, the model needs an explicit authority path that defines how ordered tests and results relate to that execution concept.
  - causal path: The causal chain is straightforward: Assay is defined separately from Test as an analyzer-performed item; the relations list never uses Assay as a from or to endpoint; therefore Assay cannot be traversed from Order, Test, Specimen, Result, or Report except through shared Department references. That makes the performed laboratory unit structurally orphaned from the workflow it is supposed to support.
  - action: Add explicit Test-to-Assay and Result-to-Assay relations, such as Test maps_to or realized_by Assay and Result produced_by Assay. If Assay is not intended to be a separate authority concept yet, merge it into Test instead. The ordering dependency is to resolve the Test-Assay catalog/execution bridge before treating the ontology as complete for EMR/LIS integration.

- issue-015 (medium): Order completion is materially under-specified because the ontology says an Order is completed when all Results are final, but it does not declare which Results belong to that Order for this rule.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, logic, structure, evolution, semantics
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 1/1 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: structure
  - remaining disagreement: 0/1 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: structure
  - issue statement: Order completion depends on all Results but the model does not structurally scope which Results belong to an Order. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml state_rules Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: Order lifecycle includes completed; Result.status values include preliminary/final/corrected; relations include Test->Result, Result->Report, Report->Order, but no Order->Result relation; state_rules says Order completes when all Result are final. Source finding context: The Order completion rule depends on Results that are not structurally scoped to an Order. Source finding context: A lifecycle rule that quantifies over 'all Result' needs a structural boundary for the result set. The current graph has Order to Report and Report to Result, plus Order to Specimen and Specimen to Result, but neither path is declared as the authoritative result scope for Order completion. In a concept authority document, that makes the completion transition structurally under-specified: different consumers could choose Report.contains Result, Specimen.produces Result, or Test.has_many Result as the source set. Source finding context: Declare the authoritative scoping relation for order completion, for example Order has_many Result via Report.contains or Specimen.produces, and update the state rule to reference that relation explicitly. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-003 Source finding context: Use the ontology as an authority for state behavior in the Order-to-Report workflow. Source finding context: When computing or implementing Order.completed, consumers must determine the set of Results for an Order without an explicit structural scope. Source finding context: Completion behavior may diverge across EMR/LIS components because the state rule depends on an unstated traversal choice. Source finding context: The lifecycle rule uses an aggregate over Result without declaring the result-set ownership path from Order. Source finding context: Order.completed is defined by all Result statuses being final. Source finding context: The relation graph has multiple indirect paths involving Order and Result but no explicit Order-to-Result scoping relation. Source finding context: Therefore the completion rule lacks a structurally unambiguous set of Results to evaluate.
  - affected purpose: Use the ontology as an authority for state behavior in the Order-to-Report workflow.
  - failure condition: Consumers must compute Order.completed and choose among indirect paths such as Report.contains Result, Specimen.produces Result, or Test.has_many Result. Source finding context: When computing or implementing Order.completed, consumers must determine the set of Results for an Order without an explicit structural scope.
  - impact: Completion behavior may diverge across EMR/LIS components because the state rule depends on an unstated traversal choice.
  - root hypothesis: The lifecycle rule aggregates over Result without declaring the authoritative result-set ownership path from Order.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-003`, `Embedded Materialized Input: entities.Order.lifecycle`, `Embedded Materialized Input: entities.Result.attributes.status`, `Embedded Materialized Input: relations list`, `Embedded Materialized Input: state_rules first item`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-019`, `finding-019.cause-001`, `finding-019.cause-002`, `Embedded Materialized Input: relations Order->Specimen, Specimen->Result, Order->Report, Report->Result, Test->Result`, `finding-019.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-015`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.evolution`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-010`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-002`, `rel-010`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: structure
  - action candidates: follow_up
  - materiality: This weakens the ontology's purpose as an authority for Order-to-Report state behavior. Consumers implementing Order.completed must choose their own traversal, such as Report.contains Result, Specimen.produces Result, or Test.has_many Result, so EMR and LIS components can compute completion differently while each appears consistent with part of the graph.
  - root cause: The root cause is the lifecycle rule's aggregate over Result without an authoritative Order-to-Result result-set ownership path. That missing scope is the starting point because the completion predicate cannot be evaluated consistently until the model defines the bounded set of Results to which all final applies.
  - causal path: The causal path is direct: Order.completed is defined by all Result statuses being final; the relation graph offers multiple indirect Order-to-Result paths and no explicit Order-to-Result scoping relation; therefore the rule lacks a structurally unambiguous result set to evaluate. Structure, logic, axiology, and coverage support this issue, while semantics and evolution narrow it to the need for a stable, declared result-set meaning and ownership path as the workflow evolves.
  - action: Declare the authoritative scoping relation for order completion and update the state rule to reference it explicitly. The action should identify whether Order's completion Results are scoped through Report.contains, Specimen.produces, another explicit Order has_many Result relation, or a defined derived path, so downstream consumers evaluate the same result set before marking an Order completed.

- issue-001: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: The issue identifies a high-severity authority and timing defect in the state model: corrected clinical information can exist while the trusted report status remains finalized. Because this weakens clinician-facing trust, it should be closed before relying on the ontology for integration design. Source finding context: The review purpose is to judge suitability as an EMR/LIS conceptual authority document. Within the target itself, the report status is explicitly presented as the trusted authority for clinicians, so accepting delayed propagation of corrected results without modeling the delay as a risk, pending state, or notification obligation weakens that purpose-critical commitment. Source finding context: Make correction propagation part of the authoritative workflow: define an immediate or bounded transition from `Result.status=corrected` to `Report.result_status=amended`, add a visible pending-correction state if batch delay is intentional, and specify clinician-facing notification/visibility requirements during the delay. Source finding context: The document's own trust promise for report status is weakened at the exact point where EMR/LIS consumers need a reliable authority for corrected clinical information. Source finding context: The ontology accepts an unstated operational tradeoff that lets batch synchronization lag behind the clinician-trusted report authority. Source finding context: The target declares `Report.result_status` to be the authoritative report status trusted by clinicians. Source finding context: The state rule allows corrected result status to update the report status only via a nightly synchronization batch. Source finding context: That delay can leave the clinician-facing authority stale during the correction window, contradicting the declared EMR/LIS authority purpose.

- issue-008: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: The missing Test-to-Assay authority threatens core order-to-execution continuity. Because that bridge is foundational for EMR/LIS integration, this high-severity issue blocks the next stage. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml: Test/Assay catalog model Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:41-55; clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:121-122 Source finding context: 신규 검사 항목 추가 시 Test와 Assay를 이중 등록해야 하며, 둘 사이의 권위 관계나 매핑 관계가 없다. Source finding context: EMR/LIS 통합의 개념 권위 문서라면 주문 카탈로그(Test)와 수행 카탈로그(Assay) 사이의 연속성이 확장 가능한 구조로 표현되어야 한다. 현재 구조에서는 새 검사, 패널 분해, 장비별 수행 코드 추가 때 동일한 의미를 두 엔티티에 수동 복제해야 하므로 코드 불일치와 누락이 생기기 쉽고, 기존 구조를 수정하지 않는 확장이 어렵다. Source finding context: Test를 주문 권위 카탈로그로 두고 Assay를 수행 구현으로 명확히 분리한 뒤 Test-to-Assay 매핑 관계를 추가한다. 신규 항목 등록 권위는 한 곳으로 정하고, 장비/부서별 변형은 매핑 엔티티나 관계 속성으로 확장되게 한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-001 Source finding context: EMR/LIS 연동 설계의 개념 권위 문서로서 주문 카탈로그와 수행 카탈로그의 확장 가능한 연결을 제공하는 목적. Source finding context: 신규 검사 항목이나 장비별 Assay가 추가될 때 Test와 Assay를 각각 등록해야 하지만 둘 사이의 권위 매핑이 없어 한쪽만 갱신되거나 의미가 갈라지는 경우. Source finding context: 주문에서 수행, 수행에서 결과로 이어지는 카탈로그 연속성이 깨져 EMR/LIS 통합 설계자가 어느 코드가 권위인지 판단할 수 없고, 신규 항목 추가가 반복될수록 재정비나 마이그레이션이 필요해진다. Source finding context: 주문 카탈로그(Test)와 수행 카탈로그(Assay)를 별도 엔티티로 만들었지만, 둘 사이의 canonical mapping 또는 단일 등록 권위를 모델링하지 않았다. Source finding context: Test는 주문 가능한 카탈로그 단위로 정의되고 Assay는 분석기 수행 단위로 별도 정의된다. Source finding context: 현재 신규 항목은 두 곳 모두에 등록한다고 명시되어 수동 이중 등록이 확장 경로가 된다. Source finding context: 두 엔티티 사이의 관계 목록에는 Test-to-Assay 연결이 없어 이중 등록된 항목의 동일성이나 변형 관계를 추적할 수 없다.

- issue-010: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: The high-severity issue describes an allowed transition that can invalidate the predicate behind a durable lifecycle state. That is a core state-model correctness problem and should be closed before next-stage use. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml state_rules: Order completion rule and Report correction rule Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml state_rules: "Order는 모든 Result가 final일 때 completed로 전이한다." and "Report는 released_at 기록 시점에 finalized 상태가 된다. 단, Result.status가 corrected로 바뀌면 Report.result_status는 amended가 되어야 한다(동기화 배치는 야간 1회)."; Result.status values: [preliminary, final, corrected]; Order.lifecycle: [draft, placed, in_progress, completed, cancelled] Source finding context: Order completion is defined from a reversible Result.status condition while the model also allows later correction of Result.status, making the completed state inconsistent after an allowed correction event. Source finding context: The conflict is formed by explicit claims only: completion requires all related Results to be final, and correction later changes at least one Result.status to corrected, which is a distinct enum value from final. Because completed is a lifecycle state with no stated retraction or amendment transition, a permitted later status change can leave an Order simultaneously treated as completed and no longer satisfying its declared completion condition. This matters for an EMR/LIS conceptual authority document because downstream integration can derive closure from Order.completed while report/result state has moved beyond the condition that justified closure. Source finding context: Define completion against a stable milestone or versioned snapshot, or add explicit post-final states/transitions such as amended/reopened and specify how Order.completed is preserved or revised when a Result becomes corrected. If corrected should still count as final for Order completion, state that explicitly, e.g. completion condition is all Results in final-or-corrected terminal statuses. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-001 Source finding context: Use of the ontology as the conceptual authority for EMR/LIS integration state behavior from Order through Report. Source finding context: A Result that was final at Order completion is later changed to corrected under the ontology's own Report correction rule. Source finding context: Integrations can observe or propagate an Order.completed state whose stated predicate is false, weakening trust in closure, downstream billing/reporting triggers, and clinician-facing workflow state. Source finding context: The completion rule uses the mutable Result.status value final as the necessary condition for a durable Order.completed lifecycle state while the same model permits later mutation of that Result.status to corrected. Source finding context: Order completion is triggered when all Results are final. Source finding context: Result.status has corrected as a separate value from final and the model allows Result.status to change to corrected after release. Source finding context: The Order lifecycle does not include a post-completion amended/reopened state or any rule preserving completed as a snapshot despite later correction.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-003: follow_up
  - rationale: The issue is a medium-severity authority gap affecting metric consistency rather than immediate clinical state correctness. It should be carried forward as a required modeling improvement during the next stage. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / turnaround_time ownership Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:6-8,126-128; .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-7 Source finding context: Delegating TAT calculation to a dashboard-owned formula drifts from the ontology's concept-authority purpose. Source finding context: TAT is defined in the target as `collected_at` to `released_at`, so letting another consumer maintain its own calculation creates a hidden local optimization: dashboards can evolve a metric separately from the concept authority that integrations are supposed to share. Source finding context: Promote TAT to an authoritative derived concept or metric rule in the ontology, name the owning timestamps and edge cases, and require downstream dashboards to consume or mirror that rule with explicit versioning. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-003 Source finding context: Use the ontology as a shared conceptual authority for EMR/LIS integration. Source finding context: Dashboard, EMR, and LIS consumers compute TAT differently because the ontology defines the metric informally while ownership remains local to the dashboard team. Source finding context: A key workflow metric can diverge across systems, reducing the ontology's usefulness as a shared authority for operational interpretation. Source finding context: The ontology defines a cross-system metric but leaves its executable ownership outside the authority document. Source finding context: The target declares itself intended as the EMR/LIS integration concept authority document. Source finding context: The notes define TAT but state the dashboard team maintains its own calculation formula. Source finding context: External metric ownership enables divergent local calculations, weakening shared concept authority.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-005: follow_up
  - rationale: This is a medium-severity lifecycle coverage gap. It affects operational completeness and audit consistency, but the projection does not frame it as blocking the immediate happy path. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / Specimen lifecycle Source finding context: Specimen.lifecycle: [collected, received, in_analysis, analyzed]; state_rules: "Specimen이 analyzed가 된 뒤의 보관/폐기 처리는 각 부서 내규를 따른다." Source finding context: Specimen lifecycle stops at analyzed and omits post-analysis storage, retention, disposal, and exception states. Source finding context: Specimen handling does not end at analysis in operational workflows. Post-analysis storage, retention, disposal, and possible retrieval/retest are lifecycle concepts that EMR/LIS integration may need to coordinate or audit. Delegating them to local policy leaves a coverage gap in the concept authority document. Source finding context: Extend Specimen lifecycle with post-analysis states such as stored, retained, disposed, rejected, lost, or recalled as appropriate, and add retention/disposal event concepts with actor, time, reason, and department policy reference if policy variability must remain. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-002 Source finding context: A complete clinical lab workflow ontology from order to report that can guide operational EMR/LIS integration. Source finding context: After Specimen reaches analyzed, the model has no canonical state or event vocabulary for storage, retention, disposal, or post-analysis exceptions. Source finding context: Systems using the ontology may diverge on specimen end-of-life semantics, making retention, disposal audit, retesting, and exception handling inconsistent across departments. Source finding context: The ontology bounds Specimen lifecycle to the analytical phase and leaves end-of-life handling outside the shared concept model. Source finding context: Specimen lifecycle enumerates only collected, received, in_analysis, and analyzed. Source finding context: The only post-analyzed handling rule says storage/disposal follows each department's internal rules, without modeling shared states or events. Source finding context: This leaves the integration authority without canonical post-analysis lifecycle concepts for specimen end-of-life operations.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-006: follow_up
  - rationale: The issue concerns historical reconstruction and audit interpretation. It is materially important for governance and reproducibility, but can be carried into the next modeling stage rather than blocking all current review closure. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / Test, Assay, CriticalValue, and TAT reference data Source finding context: Test and Assay catalog attributes lack effective dates/history; CriticalValue has bounds but no effective period; notes: "현재 신규 항목은 두 곳 모두에 등록한다" and "대시보드 팀이 자체 계산식을 유지한다." Source finding context: Time-dependent catalog, threshold, and calculation concepts lack effective-period and history coverage. Source finding context: Catalog definitions, assay mappings, critical thresholds, units, and TAT formulas are time-dependent operational facts. Without validity intervals or history, a past result/report cannot be reliably interpreted under the rules and thresholds that were in force when it was produced. Source finding context: Add versioned/effective-dated concepts for Test, Assay, CriticalValue thresholds, units/reference metadata, and TAT calculation rules. Relate Result/Report to the effective catalog or rule version used at verification/release time. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-003 Source finding context: Conceptual authority for EMR/LIS integration that supports reliable historical reconstruction of lab results and operational metrics. Source finding context: When catalog mappings, thresholds, units, or TAT formulas change, the ontology has no concept for the value version that applied at a past collection, verification, or release time. Source finding context: Historical reports, corrected results, audits, and dashboard metrics may be reinterpreted using current definitions rather than the definitions in force at the event time. Source finding context: The model represents mutable reference data as unversioned current-state entities and leaves at least one derived calculation outside the authority model. Source finding context: Test and Assay are modeled as catalog/current execution units without effective dates, version, or history attributes. Source finding context: CriticalValue thresholds are modeled as bounds for a Test but without an effective period or threshold version. Source finding context: TAT is described as a value calculated by a dashboard team's own formula, with no calculation-rule concept or version in the ontology. Source finding context: Therefore past results and metrics cannot be reconstructed against the catalog, threshold, and formula state that existed at the relevant time.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-009: follow_up
  - rationale: The issue is a medium-severity vocabulary evolution and compatibility risk. It should be carried forward to preserve extensibility and cross-system validation consistency. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml: specimen type extensibility Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:37; clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:47; clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:55 Source finding context: 검체 종류가 enum과 자유 문자열로 동시에 표현되어 새 검체 범주 추가 시 기존 구조 수정과 값 불일치가 필요해진다. Source finding context: 검체 범주는 검사 카탈로그 확장과 장비 수행 조건 변경 때 계속 세분화될 수 있다. 현재 모델은 상위 enum에는 없는 세부 검체를 Assay 문자열에만 담을 수 있어, 새 범주를 공식 수용하려면 enum 수정과 문자열 정규화가 동시에 필요하다. 이로 인해 Order-Test-Specimen-Assay 연결에서 같은 검체 의미가 서로 다른 표현으로 흩어진다. Source finding context: 검체 종류를 별도 SpecimenType 또는 SpecimenKind 카탈로그 엔티티로 승격하고 Specimen, Test, Assay가 동일한 ref를 사용하게 한다. 필요하면 parent/alias/external_code 속성으로 blood와 Serum/WB 같은 계층 및 표준 코드 변경을 흡수한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-002 Source finding context: 새 검사, 새 장비, 새 검체 처리 유형을 기존 온톨로지에 연속적으로 추가할 수 있어야 하는 확장성 목적. Source finding context: enum에 없는 세부 검체 유형이 Assay나 LIS 쪽에서 필요해질 때 Specimen/Test enum을 수정하거나 Assay 문자열과 수동 매핑해야 하는 경우. Source finding context: 확장 시 스키마와 값 체계가 함께 바뀌어 기존 데이터의 해석 연속성이 약해지고, EMR/LIS 사이에서 검체 적합성 검증이 불안정해진다. Source finding context: 검체 종류가 공유 카탈로그가 아니라 일부 필드의 폐쇄 enum과 일부 필드의 자유 문자열로 분산되어 있다. Source finding context: Specimen과 Test는 검체 종류를 네 값 enum으로 제한한다. Source finding context: Assay는 검체 종류를 문자열로 기록하며 enum보다 세분화된 예시 값을 포함한다. Source finding context: 동일한 검체 개념을 참조하는 세 필드가 하나의 권위 카탈로그를 공유하지 않으므로 새 검체 범주 추가 시 구조 변경 또는 수동 정규화가 필요하다.

- issue-011: follow_up
  - rationale: This is a narrower formal counterpart of the broader duplicated status-authority issue. It remains material as a consistency defect, but its closure can be carried with the broader status projection work. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml Result.status and Report.result_status synchronization rule Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml Result.status values: [preliminary, final, corrected]; Report.result_status values: [prelim, finalized, amended]; state_rules: "Report는 released_at 기록 시점에 finalized 상태가 된다. 단, Result.status가 corrected로 바뀌면 Report.result_status는 amended가 되어야 한다(동기화 배치는 야간 1회)." Source finding context: The rule obligates Report.result_status to be amended when any Result.status becomes corrected, but the ontology also records the same status information in two non-identical enum domains, leaving same-information synchronization under-specified as a formal constraint. Source finding context: This is not merely naming preference: the ontology asserts duplicate maintenance of the same information while declaring two different enum domains. A model instance can satisfy each enum locally, but the cross-field sameness claim lacks the explicit equivalence relation needed to satisfy the synchronization obligation for all allowed states. For an authority document, that creates conflicting implementer interpretations about which field is authoritative and when mismatched statuses are valid. Source finding context: Add a total status mapping table or unify the enum. For example: preliminary <-> prelim, final <-> finalized, corrected <-> amended, plus rules for multiple Results in one Report. Also clarify whether Report.result_status is derived from Result.status or is the authority with an explicit propagation rule. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-002 Source finding context: Conceptual authority for EMR/LIS status synchronization between LIS result state and clinician-facing report state. Source finding context: Any implementation must decide whether and how Result.status and Report.result_status represent the same status information outside the single corrected -> amended case. Source finding context: Because the ontology gives two enum sets for the same maintained information without a total equivalence relation, different systems can satisfy local enum constraints while disagreeing on valid cross-system state, undermining integration precedence and invalid-input behavior. Source finding context: The ontology splits one declared status information concept into two separate enum domains and only partially constrains their equivalence. Source finding context: Result.status is declared to be maintained also in Report.result_status as the same information. Source finding context: The two fields have non-identical enum values and Report.result_status is also described as the authority value trusted by clinicians. Source finding context: Only the corrected-to-amended synchronization case is explicitly constrained, leaving other allowed states without a formal equivalence rule.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-012: follow_up
  - rationale: The issue is a medium-severity semantic authority defect that can cause urgent-order routing and SLA divergence. It should be addressed during continued model hardening, but is not framed as an immediate blocker. Source finding context: Order.priority / Order.is_stat / StatOrder Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:16-24 Source finding context: STAT 주문 의미가 enum 값, boolean, 하위 타입으로 세 번 표현되어 동일 개념의 권위가 갈라진다. Source finding context: STAT은 주문의 우선순위/긴급도 의미인데, 이를 값·플래그·타입으로 동시에 두면 동일 주문이 `priority=stat`, `is_stat=false`, 비-`StatOrder`처럼 모순된 상태를 가질 수 있습니다. 개념 권위 문서가 이런 중복 표현을 허용하면 EMR/LIS 간 STAT 처리, 우선순위 큐, 알림 조건의 의미가 흔들립니다. Source finding context: STAT 표현을 하나의 원천으로 통합하세요. 예를 들어 `priority` enum만 권위로 두고 `is_stat`와 `StatOrder`를 제거하거나 파생 alias로 명시합니다. `StatOrder`를 유지한다면 `priority=stat` 및 `is_stat=true`와의 불변조건을 문서화하세요. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002 Source finding context: 주문 우선순위와 STAT 주문의 EMR/LIS 공통 의미 정의 Source finding context: 통합 시스템이나 규칙이 STAT 여부를 서로 다른 필드 또는 subtype에서 읽을 때 Source finding context: 동일 STAT 개념의 해석이 갈라져 긴급 검사 라우팅, SLA/TAT 계산, 알림 정책이 서로 다른 기준으로 작동할 수 있다. Source finding context: STAT을 Order의 속성값, boolean 파생값, subtype으로 동시에 모델링했다. Source finding context: `priority` enum에 `stat` 값이 포함되어 있다. Source finding context: `is_stat`도 STAT 주문 여부라고 설명한다. Source finding context: `StatOrder` 역시 응급(STAT) 검사 주문인 `Order` 하위 타입으로 정의된다. Source finding context: 세 표현 간 동치성, 우선순위, 불일치 처리 규칙이 없다.

- issue-013: follow_up
  - rationale: The relation label creates bounded provenance and role-semantics ambiguity. It is a real correctness issue for conceptual interpretation, but the projected severity supports carrying it forward rather than blocking the next stage alone. Source finding context: Specimen-to-Result 관계 의미 Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:113-114 Source finding context: `Specimen produces Result`는 검체의 의미를 결과 생산 주체로 잘못 표현한다. Source finding context: `produces`는 Order가 Report를 산출하는 관계에도 쓰입니다. 같은 동사를 검체에 적용하면 검체, 검사 수행, 분석기/assay, 결과 생성의 의미 역할이 섞입니다. EMR/LIS 통합 권위 문서에서는 검체 추적과 결과 생성 주체가 구분되어야 하므로 이 관계명은 운영 해석을 흐립니다. Source finding context: `Specimen -> Result produces`를 제거하거나 `source_for`, `used_for`, `has_result`처럼 검체의 원천 역할을 드러내는 관계로 바꾸세요. 결과 생성 주체는 `Test`/`Assay` 수행 또는 분석 이벤트 개념에 연결하는 편이 의미적으로 더 정확합니다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-003 Source finding context: 검체 추적과 결과 산출 관계의 개념 권위 Source finding context: 검체-결과 lineage, 분석 수행 주체, 결과 생성 이벤트를 이 관계명에 의존해 설계할 때 Source finding context: 검체가 행위 주체처럼 모델링되어 assay/test 수행, 장비 분석, 결과 provenance의 의미 경계가 흐려진다. Source finding context: 결과의 원천인 검체에 결과 산출 행위 동사 `produces`를 부여했다. Source finding context: `Result`는 Test 수행 결과 값으로 정의된다. Source finding context: `Assay`는 분석기에서 수행되는 검사 항목으로 정의된다. Source finding context: 그런데 관계는 `Specimen -> Result produces`와 '검체가 결과를 생산한다'는 주석을 둔다. Source finding context: 같은 `produces` 관계가 `Order -> Report`에도 쓰여 산출 주체 의미를 갖는다.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-014: follow_up
  - rationale: The orphaned Assay issue is a medium-severity structural completeness gap. It should be resolved with the Test-to-Assay mapping work, but is classified as carry-forward because the broader high-severity authority issue captures the blocking catalog bridge. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml entities.Assay Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: entities.Assay defines department ref to Department and specimen_kind, but no relation includes Assay; relations list connects Order, Specimen, Test, Result, Report, Department, Staff only. Source finding context: Assay is structurally orphaned from the workflow graph. Source finding context: From the structure lens, an entity intended to represent the performed laboratory unit should be reachable from the order-to-report graph. In the current relation set, Test connects to Result, Result connects to Report and Specimen, and Department connects to Staff, while Assay is only connected through an attribute reference to Department. That leaves no structural path that says which catalog Test maps to which analyzer Assay or which Result came from an Assay. As a concept-authority document for EMR/LIS integration, this weakens the model because a key LIS-side execution concept cannot be traversed from ordered tests or produced results. Source finding context: Add explicit relations such as Test maps_to/realized_by Assay and Result produced_by Assay, or merge Assay into Test if they are not meant to be separate authority concepts yet. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-001 Source finding context: Use the ontology as the concept authority for EMR/LIS integration from Order through Report. Source finding context: When implementers need to map ordered catalog tests to LIS/analyzer execution units or trace a result back to the performed assay, the graph has no declared Test-Assay or Result-Assay connection. Source finding context: The integration contract becomes structurally incomplete for a central cross-system mapping, so different teams may create incompatible local joins outside the authority document. Source finding context: Assay was introduced as a separate execution/catalog concept without a declared structural relation to the existing order-result-report graph. Source finding context: Assay is defined as an analyzer-performed test item and separate from Test. Source finding context: The relations section does not include Assay as either a from or to endpoint. Source finding context: Therefore Assay cannot be traversed from Order, Test, Specimen, Result, or Report except indirectly sharing Department references.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-015: follow_up
  - rationale: The missing result-set scope can cause divergent completion computation across consumers. It is a material medium-severity state-rule correctness issue, but can be carried with the related completion and correction-state modeling work. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml state_rules Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: Order lifecycle includes completed; Result.status values include preliminary/final/corrected; relations include Test->Result, Result->Report, Report->Order, but no Order->Result relation; state_rules says Order completes when all Result are final. Source finding context: The Order completion rule depends on Results that are not structurally scoped to an Order. Source finding context: A lifecycle rule that quantifies over 'all Result' needs a structural boundary for the result set. The current graph has Order to Report and Report to Result, plus Order to Specimen and Specimen to Result, but neither path is declared as the authoritative result scope for Order completion. In a concept authority document, that makes the completion transition structurally under-specified: different consumers could choose Report.contains Result, Specimen.produces Result, or Test.has_many Result as the source set. Source finding context: Declare the authoritative scoping relation for order completion, for example Order has_many Result via Report.contains or Specimen.produces, and update the state rule to reference that relation explicitly. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-003 Source finding context: Use the ontology as an authority for state behavior in the Order-to-Report workflow. Source finding context: When computing or implementing Order.completed, consumers must determine the set of Results for an Order without an explicit structural scope. Source finding context: Completion behavior may diverge across EMR/LIS components because the state rule depends on an unstated traversal choice. Source finding context: The lifecycle rule uses an aggregate over Result without declaring the result-set ownership path from Order. Source finding context: Order.completed is defined by all Result statuses being final. Source finding context: The relation graph has multiple indirect paths involving Order and Result but no explicit Order-to-Result scoping relation. Source finding context: Therefore the completion rule lacks a structurally unambiguous set of Results to evaluate.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`

### Consensus (6/6, core-axis mode)
- issue-001 (high): Corrected LIS results can leave the clinician-trusted Report.result_status stale as finalized until nightly synchronization, so the ontology is not yet reliable as an EMR/LIS authority for corrected clinical information.
  - issue stance agreement: 5/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, semantics, logic
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6

  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: structure

  - accepted lenses: axiology
  - remaining disagreement: 0/1 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: axiology
  - issue statement: Corrected LIS results can leave the clinician-trusted report status stale until nightly synchronization. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / Report.result_status correction propagation Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:80-83,121-123; .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-7 Source finding context: Nightly correction synchronization is misaligned with the ontology's stated clinician-trust commitment for report status. Source finding context: The review purpose is to judge suitability as an EMR/LIS conceptual authority document. Within the target itself, the report status is explicitly presented as the trusted authority for clinicians, so accepting delayed propagation of corrected results without modeling the delay as a risk, pending state, or notification obligation weakens that purpose-critical commitment. Source finding context: Make correction propagation part of the authoritative workflow: define an immediate or bounded transition from `Result.status=corrected` to `Report.result_status=amended`, add a visible pending-correction state if batch delay is intentional, and specify clinician-facing notification/visibility requirements during the delay. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-001 Source finding context: Use the ontology as the concept authority document for EMR/LIS integration, especially entity, relation, and state-model validity and operational risk. Source finding context: A corrected LIS result exists after report release, but the clinician-facing authoritative `Report.result_status` remains `finalized` until the nightly batch runs. Source finding context: The document's own trust promise for report status is weakened at the exact point where EMR/LIS consumers need a reliable authority for corrected clinical information. Source finding context: The ontology accepts an unstated operational tradeoff that lets batch synchronization lag behind the clinician-trusted report authority. Source finding context: The target declares `Report.result_status` to be the authoritative report status trusted by clinicians. Source finding context: The state rule allows corrected result status to update the report status only via a nightly synchronization batch. Source finding context: That delay can leave the clinician-facing authority stale during the correction window, contradicting the declared EMR/LIS authority purpose.
  - affected purpose: Use the ontology as the concept authority document for EMR/LIS integration, especially entity, relation, state-model validity, and operational risk. Source finding context: Use the ontology as the concept authority document for EMR/LIS integration, especially entity, relation, and state-model validity and operational risk.
  - failure condition: A corrected LIS result exists after report release, but Report.result_status remains finalized until the nightly batch runs. Source finding context: A corrected LIS result exists after report release, but the clinician-facing authoritative `Report.result_status` remains `finalized` until the nightly batch runs.
  - impact: The ontology weakens trust exactly where EMR/LIS consumers need reliable authority for corrected clinical information. Source finding context: The document's own trust promise for report status is weakened at the exact point where EMR/LIS consumers need a reliable authority for corrected clinical information.
  - root hypothesis: The ontology accepts batch-delayed correction propagation even though Report.result_status is declared as the clinician-facing authority.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-001`, `materialized-input.md:80-83`, `materialized-input.md:121-123`, `review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-7`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-001`, `finding-001.cause-001`, `finding-001.cause-002`, `finding-001.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-001.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-001`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-001.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-001.evolution`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-005`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-001.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-001.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-001.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-003`, `rel-011`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: axiology
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: This is material because the ontology’s declared purpose is to serve as a concept authority for EMR/LIS integration, including state-model validity and operational risk. If a corrected result exists while the clinician-facing authoritative report status still reads finalized, consumers cannot trust the modeled report status at the moment corrected clinical information matters most.
  - root cause: The root cause is the ontology’s acceptance of batch-delayed correction propagation despite declaring Report.result_status as the clinician-facing authority. That is the starting point because the stale status is not merely a display problem; it follows from an under-specified authoritative workflow that permits corrected result state to lag behind report authority.
  - causal path: The causal path is: the ontology defines Report.result_status as the trusted clinician-facing status; the correction rule allows Result.status=corrected to affect the report only through nightly synchronization; during that interval, Report.result_status can remain finalized even though corrected LIS information exists. That creates a temporary authority conflict and weakens the ontology’s own trust commitment.
  - action: Make correction propagation part of the authoritative workflow before relying on this ontology for integration design. Define an immediate or bounded transition from Result.status=corrected to Report.result_status=amended, or explicitly model a visible pending-correction state if batching remains, with clinician-facing notification and visibility requirements during any delay.

- issue-002 (high): Critical-value notification semantics are materially weakened because `CriticalValue` carries three different meanings at once: threshold/range policy, actual patient critical-result occurrence, and notification completion state.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, logic, semantics, coverage, evolution, structure

  - raised by lenses: axiology, semantics
  - issue statement: Critical-value semantics and notification auditability are weakened because threshold rules, critical-result instances, and notified state are modeled as one concept. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / CriticalValue notification model Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:100-106; .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-7 Source finding context: Critical-value handling is reduced to a boolean while the notification evidence is externalized, misaligning the model with its operational-risk purpose. Source finding context: For an EMR/LIS integration authority document, the operationally important commitment is not merely whether notification happened but whether it happened immediately, to whom, and with traceable evidence. The current model treats that evidence as outside the authority boundary while still modeling the boolean, creating an under-justified tradeoff against auditability and handoff reliability. Source finding context: Model critical-value notification as an explicit entity or relation with timestamp, recipient, notifier, channel, acknowledgement, and status; if a phone log remains authoritative, add a named relation and synchronization/ownership rule instead of a standalone boolean. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-002 Source finding context: Use the ontology to guide EMR/LIS integration around operational risks in the clinical lab workflow. Source finding context: A consuming system sees `notified=true` without authoritative recipient/time/acknowledgement data or a governed link to the phone log. Source finding context: The ontology can certify the surface completion of a critical notification while omitting the evidence needed to operate, audit, or reconcile the safety-critical workflow across EMR/LIS boundaries. Source finding context: The ontology places the evidence-bearing notification record outside the modeled authority while retaining only a lossy completion flag. Source finding context: The target defines `CriticalValue` as requiring immediate notification. Source finding context: The only modeled notification attribute is `notified: boolean`; notification time and recipient are stated to reside in a separate phone record ledger. Source finding context: This prevents the ontology from acting as a sufficient EMR/LIS authority for critical-value notification state and accountability. Source finding context: CriticalValue 엔티티 Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:95-101 Source finding context: `CriticalValue`라는 이름이 실제 모델인 위험 임계 범위 및 통보 상태와 의미적으로 맞지 않는다. Source finding context: 개별 환자 결과의 critical value와 검사별 임계 범위는 서로 다른 개념입니다. 여기에 `notified`까지 붙이면 임계 규칙, 발생한 위험 결과, 통보 이벤트가 한 엔티티 안에서 같은 의미처럼 보입니다. 전화 기록 대장에 통보 시각과 수신자가 있다는 주석도 통보가 별도 이벤트임을 시사합니다. Source finding context: 검사별 임계 범위는 `CriticalValueRange` 또는 `CriticalResultThreshold`로 명명하고, 실제 위험 결과는 `Result`를 참조하는 별도 `CriticalResult`/`CriticalAlert`로 분리하세요. `notified`는 통보 이벤트나 통보 기록 개념의 상태로 옮기고 수신자·시각 권위 위치를 명시하세요. Source finding context: 속성은 검사 참조와 하한/상한으로 임계 범위를 표현한다. Source finding context: 같은 엔티티에 `notified` boolean을 두고 통보 시각과 수신자는 별도 전화 기록 대장에 있다고 설명한다. Source finding context: 따라서 이름은 개별 critical value처럼 보이지만 실제 모델은 threshold와 notification state의 결합이다.

  - failure condition: A consuming system must interpret CriticalValue as threshold setting, patient critical result, or notification completion state, while only notified=true and external phone-log evidence are modeled. Source finding context: A consuming system sees `notified=true` without authoritative recipient/time/acknowledgement data or a governed link to the phone log. Source finding context: EMR/LIS가 이 엔티티를 critical result 인스턴스, threshold 설정, 통보 완료 상태 중 하나로 해석해야 할 때
  - impact: Critical-result notification can appear complete while omitting the evidence and semantic separation needed for audit, escalation, and clinical accountability. Source finding context: The ontology can certify the surface completion of a critical notification while omitting the evidence needed to operate, audit, or reconcile the safety-critical workflow across EMR/LIS boundaries. Source finding context: 위험 결과 알림의 대상과 규칙, 통보 상태가 섞여 환자별 critical result 추적과 통보 감사 의미가 불명확해진다.
  - root hypothesis: The model conflates critical threshold, actual critical result, and notification completion in one CriticalValue concept.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-002`, `materialized-input.md:100-106`, `review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-7`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-004`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:95-101`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-002`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-016`, `rel-003`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-003`, `finding-002.cause-001`, `materialized-input.md:100-101`, `finding-002.cause-002`, `materialized-input.md:102-106`, `finding-002.cause-003`, `finding-016.cause-001`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:95-96`, `finding-016.cause-002`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:97-100`, `finding-016.cause-003`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:101`, `finding-016.cause-004`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-002`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.structure`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-004`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: axiology, semantics
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: The declared purpose is to guide EMR/LIS integration around operational risks and critical-result notification workflow semantics. With only a `notified=true` flag and notification evidence kept outside the modeled authority, a consuming system can treat a safety-critical notification as complete without authoritative time, recipient, acknowledgement, channel, or accountable evidence needed for audit, escalation, reconciliation, and clinical handoff.
  - root cause: The starting point is the overloaded `CriticalValue` concept. A threshold rule, an actual critical result or alert, and a notification event have different identities, truth conditions, lifecycles, and evidence needs; modeling them as one concept makes downstream systems unable to know whether they are reading policy, patient-specific risk occurrence, or notification state.
  - causal path: The causal path is supported from both axiology and semantics. The target defines `CriticalValue` around immediate notification, models test reference plus lower and upper bounds as a range, then adds only `notified: boolean` while stating that notification time and recipient live in a separate phone ledger. That collapse lets the ontology expose a lossy completion flag while the evidence-bearing notification record is outside the governed authority, producing ambiguity and auditability failure across EMR/LIS boundaries.
  - action: Separate the model into at least three concepts or relations: critical threshold/range policy, actual critical result or alert tied to a patient result, and notification event/record. Move `notified` onto the notification concept or derive it from governed notification evidence, and either own timestamp, recipient, notifier, channel, acknowledgement, and status in the ontology or define an explicit authoritative relation and synchronization rule to the phone ledger. This must be fixed before the next integration stage because it is a root-level safety and governance blocker.

- issue-004 (high): The ontology must not treat critical-value notification as only a boolean. It needs an evidence-bearing notification event and an explicit workflow path from the actual result/report context to the critical-value condition and notification handling.

Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / CriticalValue notification model Source finding context: CriticalValue.attributes.notified note: "통보 완료 여부. 통보 시각과 수신자는 전화 기록 대장에 있다." Source finding context: Critical-value notification is modeled only as a boolean and omits the notification evidence concept needed for EMR/LIS authority. Source finding context: Critical-value notification is a controlled clinical action. A boolean cannot reconstruct who notified whom, when, on what basis, and whether the notice was acknowledged. For a concept authority document used in EMR/LIS integration, leaving that evidence concept outside the model weakens downstream auditability and patient-safety workflows. Source finding context: Add a CriticalValueNotification or NotificationEvent entity related to CriticalValue/Result/Order, with notifier, recipient, notified_at, channel, acknowledgement status/time, escalation fields, and source-system authority. Replace or derive notified from that event instead of making it the only modeled fact. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-001 Source finding context: Use as the conceptual authority document for EMR/LIS integration from order through report, including operational risk concepts. Source finding context: When a critical result must be communicated or audited, the ontology can only represent a completed/not-completed flag while the actor, time, recipient, and acknowledgement evidence are outside the modeled concept space. Source finding context: Implementers may integrate only a lossy boolean status, making critical-result communication non-reconstructable and weakening clinical audit, escalation, and dispute handling. Source finding context: The ontology treats critical-value notification as an attribute of the threshold concept instead of modeling the controlled notification action as its own evidence-bearing event. Source finding context: CriticalValue contains threshold fields and a boolean notified flag, but no notification event or relation to Staff/clinician/recipient. Source finding context: The only mentioned notification details, time and recipient, are described as existing in a separate phone log outside the ontology. Source finding context: Because the evidence-bearing action is outside the concept model, the ontology cannot serve as the integration authority for critical-value notification audit semantics. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml entities.CriticalValue Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: entities.CriticalValue has test_ref to Test and notified boolean, but no relation connects CriticalValue to Result, Report, Staff, Order, or notification records; relations list omits CriticalValue. Source finding context: CriticalValue is structurally disconnected from the result/report workflow. Source finding context: The structure lens checks whether necessary connections exist among declared elements. CriticalValue is not just a static catalog annotation in its definition; it includes a workflow state, notified, and says notification evidence exists in another log. Without any relation to Result or Report, the model cannot express which actual result breached a critical range or how that condition enters the order-to-report workflow. This is operationally material because critical notification is a high-risk exception path in EMR/LIS integration. Source finding context: Connect CriticalValue to the actual workflow with relations such as Result triggers CriticalValue, CriticalValue applies_to Test, CriticalValue notified_by Staff, and optionally CriticalValue documented_in NotificationRecord if notification records are in scope; otherwise remove workflow state like notified from this ontology and keep it as a pure reference range. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-002 Source finding context: Use the ontology as a concept authority for operational EMR/LIS workflow integration. Source finding context: When a result falls in a critical range and requires immediate communication, the ontology has no structural path from the Result/Report workflow to CriticalValue or notification handling. Source finding context: A critical exception process can be implemented outside the authority graph, undermining traceability and consistent integration behavior for urgent clinical notifications. Source finding context: CriticalValue was defined with workflow-relevant notification state but not attached to the workflow relation graph. Source finding context: CriticalValue represents immediately notifiable dangerous result ranges and includes notified state. Source finding context: The relation graph omits CriticalValue entirely. Source finding context: Therefore the model lacks a declared path from actual Result/Report instances to the critical value notification condition.
  - affected purpose: Use the ontology as a concept authority for operational EMR/LIS workflow integration, including critical-value notification risk. Source finding context: Use as the conceptual authority document for EMR/LIS integration from order through report, including operational risk concepts. Source finding context: Use the ontology as a concept authority for operational EMR/LIS workflow integration.
  - failure condition: A critical result must be communicated or audited, but the ontology can only represent a notified boolean and has no structural path from Result/Report to CriticalValue or notification handling. Source finding context: When a critical result must be communicated or audited, the ontology can only represent a completed/not-completed flag while the actor, time, recipient, and acknowledgement evidence are outside the modeled concept space. Source finding context: When a result falls in a critical range and requires immediate communication, the ontology has no structural path from the Result/Report workflow to CriticalValue or notification handling.
  - impact: Critical notification may be implemented outside the authority graph, weakening traceability, auditability, escalation, and consistent integration behavior. Source finding context: Implementers may integrate only a lossy boolean status, making critical-result communication non-reconstructable and weakening clinical audit, escalation, and dispute handling. Source finding context: A critical exception process can be implemented outside the authority graph, undermining traceability and consistent integration behavior for urgent clinical notifications.
  - root hypothesis: CriticalValue and notification state are kept outside the result/report workflow graph, causing both evidence coverage loss and graph disconnection.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-001`, `CriticalValue.attributes.notified note`, `CriticalValue entity lacks notification event attributes or relations`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-002`, `Embedded Materialized Input: entities.CriticalValue definition and attributes`, `Embedded Materialized Input: relations list omits CriticalValue`, `Embedded Materialized Input: CriticalValue.notified note references notification records outside the graph`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-004`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-018`, `rel-002`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-002`, `Request summary asks for operational risks around entity, relation, and state model`, `finding-004.cause-001`, `CriticalValue.attributes.test_ref/lower_bound/upper_bound/notified`, `finding-004.cause-002`, `CriticalValue.attributes.notified note: 통보 시각과 수신자는 전화 기록 대장에 있다.`, `finding-004.cause-003`, `Ontology header: EMR/LIS 연동 설계의 개념 권위 문서로 사용 예정`, `CriticalValue notification evidence omission`, `finding-018.cause-001`, `Embedded Materialized Input: entities.CriticalValue definition`, `Embedded Materialized Input: entities.CriticalValue.attributes.notified`, `finding-018.cause-002`, `Embedded Materialized Input: relations list`, `finding-018.cause-003`, `Embedded Materialized Input: entities.Result`, `Embedded Materialized Input: entities.Report`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-004`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.structure`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: coverage, structure
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: This is material because the declared purpose is to serve as the concept authority for operational EMR/LIS workflow integration, including critical-value notification risk. If a critical result must be communicated or audited, the current model can only say whether notification is complete, while actor, recipient, time, acknowledgement, escalation, and source-system evidence remain outside the authority graph. That weakens traceability, auditability, escalation, dispute handling, and consistent implementation of urgent clinical notification workflows.
  - root cause: The starting point is that CriticalValue and its notification state are kept outside the result/report workflow graph. CriticalValue carries workflow-relevant state through notified, but the controlled clinical action that would justify that state is not modeled as an evidence-bearing event, and CriticalValue is not connected to Result, Report, Staff, Order, or notification records.
  - causal path: The causal path combines the coverage and structure findings. Coverage shows that CriticalValue has threshold fields and a notified boolean, with notification time and recipient described as living in a separate phone log, so the ontology cannot reconstruct who notified whom, when, on what basis, or whether the notice was acknowledged. Structure shows that CriticalValue is omitted from the relation graph, so the model cannot express which actual Result or Report breached a critical range or how the exception enters the order-to-report workflow. Together, the missing event concept and missing graph path make critical-value notification non-reconstructable inside the integration authority.
  - action: Add a CriticalValueNotification or NotificationEvent concept and connect it to the relevant workflow objects: CriticalValue or the triggered alert, Result, Report or Order as appropriate, Staff or other notifier, recipient, acknowledgement, escalation, channel, notification time, and the authoritative notification record/source system. The notified flag should be derived from that event or replaced by it, because the evidence-bearing action must become the authority for notification completion before downstream EMR/LIS integrations rely on the model.

  - failure condition: Consumers must interpret or extend Result.status and Report.result_status across LIS and clinician-facing paths while the ontology uses separate enum names and only partial synchronization rules. Source finding context: 상태 확장이 모델 변경과 수동 매핑 변경을 요구하고, 보고서가 임상의 신뢰 값이라고 선언된 상태에서 LIS 권위 상태와 표시 상태의 일시적 또는 지속적 불일치가 발생할 수 있다.
