  - root hypothesis: Result and Report status are duplicated as separate enum carriers without a canonical mapping/projection model.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-004`, `Result.status note says LIS records and same information is maintained in Report.result_status`, `Report.result_status note says report status is the authority value clinicians trust`, `state_rules say corrected Result.status changes Report.result_status via nightly batch`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-003`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:64-78`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:116-118`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-001`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:117-118`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-007`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-010`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-013`, `rel-006`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-006`, `rel-009`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-009`, `finding-007.cause-001`, `Result.status values and note`, `finding-007.cause-002`, `Report.result_status values and note`, `finding-007.cause-003`, `state_rules second rule`, `finding-007.cause-004`, `absence of status authority/projection/sync event entity in entities and relations`, `finding-010.cause-001`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:64-68`, `finding-010.cause-002`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:75-78`, `finding-010.cause-003`, `finding-010.cause-004`, `finding-013.cause-001`, `finding-013.cause-002`, `finding-013.cause-003`, `finding-013.cause-004`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-007.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-007`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-007.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-007.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-007.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-007.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-007.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-001`, `rel-008`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: coverage, evolution, semantics
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: This weakens the ontology's declared role as EMR/LIS integration concept authority. LIS records Result.status, clinicians are told to trust Report.result_status, and nightly synchronization can leave the report-facing value stale after LIS correction. Without explicit precedence, staleness, and mapping semantics, different consumers may interpret preliminary/final/corrected display, completion, and amendment propagation differently.
  - root cause: The starting point is that Result and Report each carry a status vocabulary for the same or overlapping status information, but no canonical status concept or authoritative mapping/projection model defines which value is source authority, which is derived, how aliases relate, or how changes evolve over time.
  - causal path: The causal path is supported across coverage, evolution, and semantics findings: Result.status is LIS-recorded with preliminary/final/corrected values; Report.result_status separately uses prelim/finalized/amended and is described as the clinician-trusted authority; the only modeled connection is a nightly corrected-to-amended synchronization rule. That leaves a gap for authority, freshness, total mapping, and status transition semantics, so locally valid Result and Report instances can become globally inconsistent or stale.
  - action: Fix this before the next stage by introducing either a canonical ResultStatus concept or an explicit StatusMapping/projection model. The model should define source authority, report-facing projection, clinical display value, alias or distinct-state semantics, freshness or last_synced_at, synchronization/correction events, update authority, terminal status, and transition rules so future LIS states or reporting-policy changes extend the catalog rather than requiring coordinated enum and prose-rule edits.

This is a high-severity root-cause issue because the order catalog and execution catalog can diverge as the catalog evolves.

Source finding context: EMR/LIS 통합의 개념 권위 문서라면 주문 카탈로그(Test)와 수행 카탈로그(Assay) 사이의 연속성이 확장 가능한 구조로 표현되어야 한다. 현재 구조에서는 새 검사, 패널 분해, 장비별 수행 코드 추가 때 동일한 의미를 두 엔티티에 수동 복제해야 하므로 코드 불일치와 누락이 생기기 쉽고, 기존 구조를 수정하지 않는 확장이 어렵다. Source finding context: Test를 주문 권위 카탈로그로 두고 Assay를 수행 구현으로 명확히 분리한 뒤 Test-to-Assay 매핑 관계를 추가한다. 신규 항목 등록 권위는 한 곳으로 정하고, 장비/부서별 변형은 매핑 엔티티나 관계 속성으로 확장되게 한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-001 Source finding context: EMR/LIS 연동 설계의 개념 권위 문서로서 주문 카탈로그와 수행 카탈로그의 확장 가능한 연결을 제공하는 목적. Source finding context: 신규 검사 항목이나 장비별 Assay가 추가될 때 Test와 Assay를 각각 등록해야 하지만 둘 사이의 권위 매핑이 없어 한쪽만 갱신되거나 의미가 갈라지는 경우. Source finding context: 주문에서 수행, 수행에서 결과로 이어지는 카탈로그 연속성이 깨져 EMR/LIS 통합 설계자가 어느 코드가 권위인지 판단할 수 없고, 신규 항목 추가가 반복될수록 재정비나 마이그레이션이 필요해진다. Source finding context: 주문 카탈로그(Test)와 수행 카탈로그(Assay)를 별도 엔티티로 만들었지만, 둘 사이의 canonical mapping 또는 단일 등록 권위를 모델링하지 않았다. Source finding context: Test는 주문 가능한 카탈로그 단위로 정의되고 Assay는 분석기 수행 단위로 별도 정의된다. Source finding context: 현재 신규 항목은 두 곳 모두에 등록한다고 명시되어 수동 이중 등록이 확장 경로가 된다. Source finding context: 두 엔티티 사이의 관계 목록에는 Test-to-Assay 연결이 없어 이중 등록된 항목의 동일성이나 변형 관계를 추적할 수 없다.
  - affected purpose: EMR/LIS integration authority for extensible continuity between order catalog and execution catalog. Source finding context: EMR/LIS 연동 설계의 개념 권위 문서로서 주문 카탈로그와 수행 카탈로그의 확장 가능한 연결을 제공하는 목적.
  - failure condition: A new test item or analyzer-specific assay is added, but Test and Assay must be registered separately without an authoritative mapping. Source finding context: 신규 검사 항목이나 장비별 Assay가 추가될 때 Test와 Assay를 각각 등록해야 하지만 둘 사이의 권위 매핑이 없어 한쪽만 갱신되거나 의미가 갈라지는 경우.
  - impact: Catalog continuity from order to execution to result can break, leaving implementers unable to identify the authoritative code or relation. Source finding context: 주문에서 수행, 수행에서 결과로 이어지는 카탈로그 연속성이 깨져 EMR/LIS 통합 설계자가 어느 코드가 권위인지 판단할 수 없고, 신규 항목 추가가 반복될수록 재정비나 마이그레이션이 필요해진다.
  - root hypothesis: Test and Assay are separate catalog/execution concepts without a canonical mapping or single registration authority.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-001`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:41-55`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:121-122`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-008`, `finding-008.cause-001`, `finding-008.cause-002`, `finding-008.cause-003`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:103-114`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-008.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-008`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-008.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-008.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-008.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-008.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-008.structure`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-013`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-005`, `rel-014`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: evolution
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: The ontology is meant to act as an EMR/LIS integration authority for extensible continuity from ordered tests to executed assays and onward to results. Without an authoritative bridge between Test and Assay, implementers cannot reliably tell which code or relationship governs a new item, panel decomposition, or analyzer-specific execution variant, so catalog continuity can break and later migration or rework becomes likely.
  - root cause: The causal chain starts with Test and Assay being modeled as separate concepts: Test is the orderable catalog unit, while Assay is the analyzer execution unit. That separation is valid only if the ontology also defines the canonical mapping or ownership rule between them; because it does not, the model leaves identity, variant handling, and registration authority outside the authority graph.
  - causal path: The evidence path is that Test and Assay are separately defined, new items are described as needing registration in both places, and the relationship list lacks a Test-to-Assay connection. That makes manual dual entry the extension path, which enables one side to be updated without the other or for meanings to drift between order and execution catalogs.
  - action: Make Test the authoritative order catalog concept and Assay the execution implementation concept, then add an authoritative Test-to-Assay mapping or relation. New-item registration ownership should be assigned to one authority, while analyzer, department, panel, or implementation variants should be represented through the mapping entity or relation properties so extensions do not require semantic duplication.

- issue-010 (high): Order.completed is materially inconsistent because the ontology allows a completed Order to remain completed after a later Result correction changes Result.status from final to corrected, making the stated completion predicate false.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, logic, semantics, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 1/1 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: logic
  - remaining disagreement: 0/1 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: logic
  - issue statement: Order.completed can remain true after a later allowed correction makes the stated completion predicate false. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml state_rules: Order completion rule and Report correction rule Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml state_rules: "Order는 모든 Result가 final일 때 completed로 전이한다." and "Report는 released_at 기록 시점에 finalized 상태가 된다. 단, Result.status가 corrected로 바뀌면 Report.result_status는 amended가 되어야 한다(동기화 배치는 야간 1회)."; Result.status values: [preliminary, final, corrected]; Order.lifecycle: [draft, placed, in_progress, completed, cancelled] Source finding context: Order completion is defined from a reversible Result.status condition while the model also allows later correction of Result.status, making the completed state inconsistent after an allowed correction event. Source finding context: The conflict is formed by explicit claims only: completion requires all related Results to be final, and correction later changes at least one Result.status to corrected, which is a distinct enum value from final. Because completed is a lifecycle state with no stated retraction or amendment transition, a permitted later status change can leave an Order simultaneously treated as completed and no longer satisfying its declared completion condition. This matters for an EMR/LIS conceptual authority document because downstream integration can derive closure from Order.completed while report/result state has moved beyond the condition that justified closure. Source finding context: Define completion against a stable milestone or versioned snapshot, or add explicit post-final states/transitions such as amended/reopened and specify how Order.completed is preserved or revised when a Result becomes corrected. If corrected should still count as final for Order completion, state that explicitly, e.g. completion condition is all Results in final-or-corrected terminal statuses. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-001 Source finding context: Use of the ontology as the conceptual authority for EMR/LIS integration state behavior from Order through Report. Source finding context: A Result that was final at Order completion is later changed to corrected under the ontology's own Report correction rule. Source finding context: Integrations can observe or propagate an Order.completed state whose stated predicate is false, weakening trust in closure, downstream billing/reporting triggers, and clinician-facing workflow state. Source finding context: The completion rule uses the mutable Result.status value final as the necessary condition for a durable Order.completed lifecycle state while the same model permits later mutation of that Result.status to corrected. Source finding context: Order completion is triggered when all Results are final. Source finding context: Result.status has corrected as a separate value from final and the model allows Result.status to change to corrected after release. Source finding context: The Order lifecycle does not include a post-completion amended/reopened state or any rule preserving completed as a snapshot despite later correction.
  - affected purpose: Use of the ontology as the conceptual authority for EMR/LIS integration state behavior from Order through Report.
  - failure condition: A Result that was final at Order completion is later changed to corrected under the ontology's own correction rule. Source finding context: A Result that was final at Order completion is later changed to corrected under the ontology's own Report correction rule.
  - impact: Integrations can observe or propagate an Order.completed state whose stated predicate is false, weakening closure, billing/reporting triggers, and workflow trust. Source finding context: Integrations can observe or propagate an Order.completed state whose stated predicate is false, weakening trust in closure, downstream billing/reporting triggers, and clinician-facing workflow state.
  - root hypothesis: The durable Order.completed lifecycle state is derived from mutable Result.status values without post-completion correction semantics.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-001`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml Order.lifecycle: [draft, placed, in_progress, completed, cancelled]`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml Result.status values: [preliminary, final, corrected]`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml state_rules: "Order는 모든 Result가 final일 때 completed로 전이한다."`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml state_rules: "Result.status가 corrected로 바뀌면 Report.result_status는 amended가 되어야 한다"`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-011`, `finding-011.cause-001`, `finding-011.cause-002`, `finding-011.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-010`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.evolution`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-011`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.structure`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-010`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-002`, `rel-010`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-003`, `rel-011`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: logic
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: This weakens the ontology's purpose as the conceptual authority for EMR/LIS integration state behavior from Order through Report. Downstream systems may treat Order.completed as authoritative closure for workflow, billing, reporting, or clinician-facing state even though the result state has moved beyond the condition that justified completion.
  - root cause: The starting point is that a durable Order.completed lifecycle state is derived from mutable Result.status values. The model says completion occurs when all Results are final, but it also permits a later correction that changes Result.status to corrected without defining whether completed is preserved as a snapshot, reopened, amended, or recalculated.
  - causal path: The causal path is explicit: Order completion is triggered when all Results are final; corrected is a separate Result.status value from final; the correction rule allows Result.status to become corrected after release; and the Order lifecycle only lists draft, placed, in_progress, completed, and cancelled, with no post-completion amended/reopened state or snapshot rule. Therefore an allowed correction can leave Order.completed true while the predicate 'all Results are final' is no longer true.
  - action: Fix this before the next stage by defining completion against a stable milestone or versioned snapshot, or by adding explicit corrected/amended/reopened semantics and terminal-state rules. If corrected Results should still satisfy completion, the ontology must state that final-or-corrected terminal statuses count for Order completion; otherwise it must define how Order.completed changes or is qualified after correction.

- issue-003 (medium): TAT should be treated as a material authority gap: the ontology names a cross-system turnaround-time metric, but its executable calculation remains informally described and externally owned, so dashboard, EMR, and LIS consumers can drift apart.
  - issue stance agreement: 5/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, logic, semantics
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: structure
  - resolution accepted by: 1/1 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: axiology
  - remaining disagreement: 0/1 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: axiology
  - issue statement: TAT can drift across dashboard, EMR, and LIS consumers because its formula is described informally while owned externally. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / turnaround_time ownership Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:6-8,126-128; .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-7 Source finding context: Delegating TAT calculation to a dashboard-owned formula drifts from the ontology's concept-authority purpose. Source finding context: TAT is defined in the target as `collected_at` to `released_at`, so letting another consumer maintain its own calculation creates a hidden local optimization: dashboards can evolve a metric separately from the concept authority that integrations are supposed to share. Source finding context: Promote TAT to an authoritative derived concept or metric rule in the ontology, name the owning timestamps and edge cases, and require downstream dashboards to consume or mirror that rule with explicit versioning. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-003 Source finding context: Use the ontology as a shared conceptual authority for EMR/LIS integration. Source finding context: Dashboard, EMR, and LIS consumers compute TAT differently because the ontology defines the metric informally while ownership remains local to the dashboard team. Source finding context: A key workflow metric can diverge across systems, reducing the ontology's usefulness as a shared authority for operational interpretation. Source finding context: The ontology defines a cross-system metric but leaves its executable ownership outside the authority document. Source finding context: The target declares itself intended as the EMR/LIS integration concept authority document. Source finding context: The notes define TAT but state the dashboard team maintains its own calculation formula. Source finding context: External metric ownership enables divergent local calculations, weakening shared concept authority.
  - affected purpose: Use the ontology as a shared conceptual authority for EMR/LIS integration.
  - failure condition: Dashboard, EMR, and LIS consumers compute TAT differently because the ontology defines the metric informally while formula ownership remains local to the dashboard team. Source finding context: Dashboard, EMR, and LIS consumers compute TAT differently because the ontology defines the metric informally while ownership remains local to the dashboard team.
  - impact: A key workflow metric can diverge across systems, reducing shared operational interpretation. Source finding context: A key workflow metric can diverge across systems, reducing the ontology's usefulness as a shared authority for operational interpretation.
  - root hypothesis: The ontology defines a cross-system TAT metric but leaves executable calculation ownership outside the authority document.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-003`, `materialized-input.md:6-8`, `materialized-input.md:126-128`, `review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-7`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-003`, `finding-003.cause-001`, `finding-003.cause-002`, `finding-003.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-003.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-003.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-003.evolution`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-012`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-003.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-003.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-003.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-004`, `rel-012`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: axiology
  - action candidates: follow_up
  - materiality: This weakens the declared purpose because the ontology is meant to serve as the shared conceptual authority for EMR/LIS integration. If TAT is interpreted or calculated locally by downstream consumers, a key operational metric no longer has one shared meaning across systems, reducing consistent operational interpretation.
  - root cause: The root cause is that the ontology defines TAT as a cross-system metric while leaving the authoritative calculation rule outside the ontology. That is the starting point because downstream divergence becomes possible only after the shared authority fails to own the derivation, edge cases, and versioned rule for the metric.
  - causal path: The causal path is: the target declares itself the EMR/LIS integration concept authority; the notes define TAT but say the dashboard team maintains its own formula; that external ownership allows local calculation changes outside the shared authority. Logic and semantics narrowed the concern to the absence of a formal authoritative derivation rule, while other supporting lenses kept the medium authority-drift issue intact.
  - action: Promote TAT to an authoritative derived concept or versioned metric rule in the ontology. The rule should name the owning timestamps, calculation semantics, edge cases, and downstream consumption or mirroring expectations so dashboards, EMR, and LIS integrations all depend on the same governed metric definition.

- issue-005 (medium): Specimen lifecycle coverage is materially incomplete because the shared ontology stops at analyzed and does not define post-analysis storage, retention, disposal, retrieval, or exception states.
  - issue stance agreement: 3/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: logic, structure
  - insufficient evidence lenses: semantics
  - resolution accepted by: 1/1 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: coverage
  - remaining disagreement: 0/1 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: coverage
  - issue statement: Specimen storage, retention, disposal, retrieval, and exception states are omitted after analyzed. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / Specimen lifecycle Source finding context: Specimen.lifecycle: [collected, received, in_analysis, analyzed]; state_rules: "Specimen이 analyzed가 된 뒤의 보관/폐기 처리는 각 부서 내규를 따른다." Source finding context: Specimen lifecycle stops at analyzed and omits post-analysis storage, retention, disposal, and exception states. Source finding context: Specimen handling does not end at analysis in operational workflows. Post-analysis storage, retention, disposal, and possible retrieval/retest are lifecycle concepts that EMR/LIS integration may need to coordinate or audit. Delegating them to local policy leaves a coverage gap in the concept authority document. Source finding context: Extend Specimen lifecycle with post-analysis states such as stored, retained, disposed, rejected, lost, or recalled as appropriate, and add retention/disposal event concepts with actor, time, reason, and department policy reference if policy variability must remain. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-002 Source finding context: A complete clinical lab workflow ontology from order to report that can guide operational EMR/LIS integration. Source finding context: After Specimen reaches analyzed, the model has no canonical state or event vocabulary for storage, retention, disposal, or post-analysis exceptions. Source finding context: Systems using the ontology may diverge on specimen end-of-life semantics, making retention, disposal audit, retesting, and exception handling inconsistent across departments. Source finding context: The ontology bounds Specimen lifecycle to the analytical phase and leaves end-of-life handling outside the shared concept model. Source finding context: Specimen lifecycle enumerates only collected, received, in_analysis, and analyzed. Source finding context: The only post-analyzed handling rule says storage/disposal follows each department's internal rules, without modeling shared states or events. Source finding context: This leaves the integration authority without canonical post-analysis lifecycle concepts for specimen end-of-life operations.
  - affected purpose: A complete clinical lab workflow ontology from order to report that can guide operational EMR/LIS integration.
  - failure condition: After Specimen reaches analyzed, the model has no canonical state or event vocabulary for storage, retention, disposal, or post-analysis exceptions.
  - impact: Systems may diverge on specimen end-of-life semantics, weakening retention, disposal audit, retesting, and exception handling. Source finding context: Systems using the ontology may diverge on specimen end-of-life semantics, making retention, disposal audit, retesting, and exception handling inconsistent across departments.
  - root hypothesis: The ontology bounds Specimen lifecycle to the analytical phase and leaves end-of-life handling outside the shared concept model.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-002`, `Specimen.lifecycle: [collected, received, in_analysis, analyzed]`, `state_rules post-analysis storage/disposal delegated to department policy`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-005`, `finding-005.cause-001`, `Specimen.lifecycle`, `finding-005.cause-002`, `state_rules post-analysis storage/disposal sentence`, `finding-005.cause-003`, `Ontology header declares concept model for hospital lab pipeline and EMR/LIS integration authority`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-005`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.structure`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: coverage
  - action candidates: follow_up
  - materiality: The declared purpose is to guide operational EMR/LIS integration across the clinical lab workflow. When specimen end-of-life handling is left outside the canonical model, departments and systems can invent incompatible meanings for retention, disposal audit, retesting, retrieval, rejected, lost, or recalled specimens, weakening cross-system consistency after analysis.
  - root cause: The root cause starts at the ontology boundary: Specimen lifecycle is modeled only through the analytical phase, with collected, received, in_analysis, and analyzed as the listed states. Because post-analysis handling is delegated to department policy instead of represented as shared lifecycle states or events, the integration authority has no canonical concepts for specimen end-of-life operations.
  - causal path: The causal path is direct: the lifecycle enumeration ends at analyzed; the only post-analyzed rule says storage and disposal follow each department's internal rules; therefore the shared concept model provides no common vocabulary for retention, disposal, retrieval, retesting, or exception handling. Coverage, axiology, and evolution support the gap; logic and structure treat it as outside their lens scope, and semantics has insufficient lens-specific evidence rather than opposition.
  - action: Extend the Specimen lifecycle with governed post-analysis states such as stored, retained, disposed, rejected, lost, or recalled as appropriate, and add retention/disposal event concepts with actor, time, reason, and department policy reference where policy variability must remain. This should follow the existing lifecycle authority so local policies can vary without removing shared audit and integration semantics.

- issue-006 (medium): Catalog, threshold, unit/reference, and TAT rule facts are time-dependent but modeled as current-state concepts, so historical lab results and operational metrics cannot be reliably reconstructed under the definitions that were in force when the events occurred.
  - issue stance agreement: 5/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, logic, semantics
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none

  - remaining disagreement: 0/1 deliberation participants

  - issue statement: Catalogs, thresholds, units, and TAT rules lack effective-period and history coverage for historical reconstruction. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / Test, Assay, CriticalValue, and TAT reference data Source finding context: Test and Assay catalog attributes lack effective dates/history; CriticalValue has bounds but no effective period; notes: "현재 신규 항목은 두 곳 모두에 등록한다" and "대시보드 팀이 자체 계산식을 유지한다." Source finding context: Time-dependent catalog, threshold, and calculation concepts lack effective-period and history coverage. Source finding context: Catalog definitions, assay mappings, critical thresholds, units, and TAT formulas are time-dependent operational facts. Without validity intervals or history, a past result/report cannot be reliably interpreted under the rules and thresholds that were in force when it was produced. Source finding context: Add versioned/effective-dated concepts for Test, Assay, CriticalValue thresholds, units/reference metadata, and TAT calculation rules. Relate Result/Report to the effective catalog or rule version used at verification/release time. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-003 Source finding context: Conceptual authority for EMR/LIS integration that supports reliable historical reconstruction of lab results and operational metrics. Source finding context: When catalog mappings, thresholds, units, or TAT formulas change, the ontology has no concept for the value version that applied at a past collection, verification, or release time. Source finding context: Historical reports, corrected results, audits, and dashboard metrics may be reinterpreted using current definitions rather than the definitions in force at the event time. Source finding context: The model represents mutable reference data as unversioned current-state entities and leaves at least one derived calculation outside the authority model. Source finding context: Test and Assay are modeled as catalog/current execution units without effective dates, version, or history attributes. Source finding context: CriticalValue thresholds are modeled as bounds for a Test but without an effective period or threshold version. Source finding context: TAT is described as a value calculated by a dashboard team's own formula, with no calculation-rule concept or version in the ontology. Source finding context: Therefore past results and metrics cannot be reconstructed against the catalog, threshold, and formula state that existed at the relevant time.

Source finding context: When catalog mappings, thresholds, units, or TAT formulas change, the ontology has no concept for the value version that applied at a past collection, verification, or release time.
  - impact: Historical reports, corrected results, audits, and dashboard metrics may be interpreted under current definitions instead of event-time definitions. Source finding context: Historical reports, corrected results, audits, and dashboard metrics may be reinterpreted using current definitions rather than the definitions in force at the event time.
  - root hypothesis: Mutable reference data is modeled as unversioned current-state entities and at least one derived calculation is outside the authority model.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-003`, `Test attributes lack validity/version fields`, `Assay attributes lack validity/version fields`, `CriticalValue lower_bound/upper_bound lack effective period`, `notes mention dual Test/Assay registration and external TAT calculation`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-006`, `finding-006.cause-001`, `Test.attributes`, `Assay.attributes`, `finding-006.cause-002`, `CriticalValue.attributes.lower_bound/upper_bound/test_ref`, `finding-006.cause-003`, `notes: turnaround_time(TAT)은 collected_at부터 released_at까지로 계산하며, 대시보드 팀이 자체 계산식을 유지한다.`, `finding-006.cause-004`, `absence of effective/history fields across these entities and notes`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-006.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-006`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-006.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-006.evolution`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-014`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-006.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-006.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-006.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-004`, `rel-012`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-005`, `rel-014`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/problem-framing.yaml`

If catalog mappings, critical thresholds, units, or TAT formulas change without effective-period or version authority, old reports, corrected results, audits, and dashboards may be interpreted using current definitions rather than event-time definitions.
  - root cause: The starting point is that mutable reference data is not modeled as versioned or effective-dated authority, and at least one derived calculation, TAT, remains outside the ontology-owned authority model. Without that root authority, downstream results and reports have nothing stable to reference for the rules that applied at collection, verification, or release time.
  - causal path: The causal path is direct: Test and Assay are catalog/current execution entities without validity, version, or history fields; CriticalValue stores bounds for a Test without an effective period or threshold version; and TAT is described as calculated by an external dashboard formula rather than by a versioned rule concept. Together, these omissions mean past results and metrics cannot be tied back to the catalog, threshold, unit, and formula state that governed them at the relevant event time.
  - action: Add versioned or effective-dated concepts for Test, Assay, CriticalValue thresholds, unit/reference metadata, and TAT calculation rules, then relate Result and Report records to the effective catalog or rule version used at verification or release time. The ordering matters: first establish the versioned authority concepts, then wire historical result/report interpretation to those versions so reconstruction uses event-time rules instead of current-state defaults.

- issue-009 (medium): Specimen compatibility vocabulary can diverge because Specimen and Test use closed specimen-kind enums while Assay stores specimen kind as a free string. This should be carried forward as a material medium-severity extensibility issue.

Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml: specimen type extensibility Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:37; clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:47; clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:55 Source finding context: 검체 종류가 enum과 자유 문자열로 동시에 표현되어 새 검체 범주 추가 시 기존 구조 수정과 값 불일치가 필요해진다. Source finding context: 검체 범주는 검사 카탈로그 확장과 장비 수행 조건 변경 때 계속 세분화될 수 있다. 현재 모델은 상위 enum에는 없는 세부 검체를 Assay 문자열에만 담을 수 있어, 새 범주를 공식 수용하려면 enum 수정과 문자열 정규화가 동시에 필요하다. 이로 인해 Order-Test-Specimen-Assay 연결에서 같은 검체 의미가 서로 다른 표현으로 흩어진다. Source finding context: 검체 종류를 별도 SpecimenType 또는 SpecimenKind 카탈로그 엔티티로 승격하고 Specimen, Test, Assay가 동일한 ref를 사용하게 한다. 필요하면 parent/alias/external_code 속성으로 blood와 Serum/WB 같은 계층 및 표준 코드 변경을 흡수한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-002 Source finding context: 새 검사, 새 장비, 새 검체 처리 유형을 기존 온톨로지에 연속적으로 추가할 수 있어야 하는 확장성 목적. Source finding context: enum에 없는 세부 검체 유형이 Assay나 LIS 쪽에서 필요해질 때 Specimen/Test enum을 수정하거나 Assay 문자열과 수동 매핑해야 하는 경우. Source finding context: 확장 시 스키마와 값 체계가 함께 바뀌어 기존 데이터의 해석 연속성이 약해지고, EMR/LIS 사이에서 검체 적합성 검증이 불안정해진다. Source finding context: 검체 종류가 공유 카탈로그가 아니라 일부 필드의 폐쇄 enum과 일부 필드의 자유 문자열로 분산되어 있다. Source finding context: Specimen과 Test는 검체 종류를 네 값 enum으로 제한한다. Source finding context: Assay는 검체 종류를 문자열로 기록하며 enum보다 세분화된 예시 값을 포함한다. Source finding context: 동일한 검체 개념을 참조하는 세 필드가 하나의 권위 카탈로그를 공유하지 않으므로 새 검체 범주 추가 시 구조 변경 또는 수동 정규화가 필요하다.
  - affected purpose: Extensibility for adding new tests, devices, and specimen handling types without breaking ontology continuity. Source finding context: 새 검사, 새 장비, 새 검체 처리 유형을 기존 온톨로지에 연속적으로 추가할 수 있어야 하는 확장성 목적.
  - failure condition: A detailed specimen type absent from the enum is needed by Assay or LIS and must be represented through enum changes or manual string normalization. Source finding context: enum에 없는 세부 검체 유형이 Assay나 LIS 쪽에서 필요해질 때 Specimen/Test enum을 수정하거나 Assay 문자열과 수동 매핑해야 하는 경우.
  - impact: Schema and value systems change together, weakening historical interpretation and cross-system specimen compatibility validation. Source finding context: 확장 시 스키마와 값 체계가 함께 바뀌어 기존 데이터의 해석 연속성이 약해지고, EMR/LIS 사이에서 검체 적합성 검증이 불안정해진다.
  - root hypothesis: Specimen kind is split across closed enums and free strings instead of a shared authoritative specimen-kind catalog.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-002`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:37`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:47`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:55`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-009`, `finding-009.cause-001`, `finding-009.cause-002`, `finding-009.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-009`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.structure`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-015`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-006`, `rel-015`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: evolution
  - action candidates: follow_up
  - materiality: The declared purpose is to add new tests, devices, and specimen handling types without breaking ontology continuity. When a more detailed specimen type is needed, the model must either change the Specimen/Test enum or normalize Assay/LIS strings manually, so schema evolution and value interpretation become coupled and cross-system compatibility checks become less reliable.
  - root cause: The starting point is the absence of one authoritative SpecimenKind or SpecimenType catalog. Because the same domain concept is represented separately as closed enum values and free-text strings, later specimen refinements do not have a stable shared identity that Specimen, Test, and Assay can all reference.
  - causal path: The causal path is supported by finding-009: Specimen and Test restrict specimen kind to a small enum, Assay records specimen kind as a string with more detailed example values, and those fields therefore compare across different value domains. This creates vocabulary drift when new specimen categories appear, because the ontology must either modify enum structure or rely on manual mapping between enum values and strings.
  - action: Promote specimen kind into a shared SpecimenKind or SpecimenType catalog and have Specimen, Test, and Assay reference it. The catalog should support aliases, hierarchy, or external codes where needed so variants such as broad blood categories and more specific serum or whole-blood terms can evolve without rewriting each consuming concept.

- issue-011 (medium): Result.status and Report.result_status are presented as maintaining the same status information, but the ontology defines separate enum domains and only specifies the corrected -> amended synchronization case. This leaves the overall equivalence and authority relationship under-specified.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, logic, semantics, evolution, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 1/1 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: logic
  - remaining disagreement: 0/1 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: logic
  - issue statement: Result.status and Report.result_status claim to maintain the same information but lack a total equivalence rule. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml Result.status and Report.result_status synchronization rule Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml Result.status values: [preliminary, final, corrected]; Report.result_status values: [prelim, finalized, amended]; state_rules: "Report는 released_at 기록 시점에 finalized 상태가 된다. 단, Result.status가 corrected로 바뀌면 Report.result_status는 amended가 되어야 한다(동기화 배치는 야간 1회)." Source finding context: The rule obligates Report.result_status to be amended when any Result.status becomes corrected, but the ontology also records the same status information in two non-identical enum domains, leaving same-information synchronization under-specified as a formal constraint. Source finding context: This is not merely naming preference: the ontology asserts duplicate maintenance of the same information while declaring two different enum domains. A model instance can satisfy each enum locally, but the cross-field sameness claim lacks the explicit equivalence relation needed to satisfy the synchronization obligation for all allowed states. For an authority document, that creates conflicting implementer interpretations about which field is authoritative and when mismatched statuses are valid. Source finding context: Add a total status mapping table or unify the enum. For example: preliminary <-> prelim, final <-> finalized, corrected <-> amended, plus rules for multiple Results in one Report. Also clarify whether Report.result_status is derived from Result.status or is the authority with an explicit propagation rule. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-002 Source finding context: Conceptual authority for EMR/LIS status synchronization between LIS result state and clinician-facing report state. Source finding context: Any implementation must decide whether and how Result.status and Report.result_status represent the same status information outside the single corrected -> amended case. Source finding context: Because the ontology gives two enum sets for the same maintained information without a total equivalence relation, different systems can satisfy local enum constraints while disagreeing on valid cross-system state, undermining integration precedence and invalid-input behavior. Source finding context: The ontology splits one declared status information concept into two separate enum domains and only partially constrains their equivalence. Source finding context: Result.status is declared to be maintained also in Report.result_status as the same information. Source finding context: The two fields have non-identical enum values and Report.result_status is also described as the authority value trusted by clinicians. Source finding context: Only the corrected-to-amended synchronization case is explicitly constrained, leaving other allowed states without a formal equivalence rule.
  - affected purpose: Conceptual authority for EMR/LIS status synchronization between LIS result state and clinician-facing report state.
  - failure condition: An implementation must decide whether and how Result.status and Report.result_status represent the same status information outside the corrected-to-amended case. Source finding context: Any implementation must decide whether and how Result.status and Report.result_status represent the same status information outside the single corrected -> amended case.
  - impact: Different systems can satisfy local enum constraints while disagreeing on valid cross-system state, undermining integration precedence and invalid-input behavior. Source finding context: Because the ontology gives two enum sets for the same maintained information without a total equivalence relation, different systems can satisfy local enum constraints while disagreeing on valid cross-system state, undermining integration precedence and invalid-input behavior.
  - root hypothesis: The ontology splits one declared status information concept into two enum domains and only partially constrains equivalence.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-002`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml Result.status note: "Report.result_status에도 동일 정보가 유지된다."`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml Result.status values: [preliminary, final, corrected]`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml Report.result_status values: [prelim, finalized, amended]`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml Report.result_status note: "보고서에 표시되는 결과 상태의 권위 값."`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml state_rules correction sentence`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-012`, `finding-012.cause-001`, `finding-012.cause-002`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml Report.result_status note: "보고서에 표시되는 결과 상태의 권위 값. 임상의는 이 값을 신뢰한다."`, `finding-012.cause-003`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml state_rules: "Result.status가 corrected로 바뀌면 Report.result_status는 amended가 되어야 한다"`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-011`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.evolution`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-008`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-001`, `rel-008`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: logic
  - action candidates: follow_up
  - materiality: The affected purpose is conceptual authority for EMR/LIS status synchronization between LIS result state and clinician-facing report state. Without a total mapping or a single authoritative enum, implementations can each satisfy local enum constraints while disagreeing about which cross-field status combinations are valid, which field controls, and how invalid mismatches should be handled.
  - root cause: The starting point is the split of one declared status information concept into two enum domains: Result.status uses preliminary/final/corrected, while Report.result_status uses prelim/finalized/amended and is also clinician-facing authority. Because the ontology says the same information is maintained in both places, the split requires an explicit equivalence or projection rule; only a partial rule is present.
  - causal path: The causal path is: Result.status is declared to be maintained in Report.result_status as the same information; the two fields then use non-identical enum values and Report.result_status is described as the trusted report value; finally, the state rule only requires corrected Result.status to produce amended Report.result_status. That supports the issue because preliminary/prelim and final/finalized, as well as multi-result report aggregation and authority direction, remain formally unconstrained.
  - action: Add a total status mapping table or unify the enum. If two fields remain, clarify whether Report.result_status is derived from Result.status or is authoritative, define the propagation rule, and include rules for multiple Results in one Report so every allowed state has a determinate cross-field interpretation.

- issue-012 (medium): STAT order meaning is materially ambiguous because the ontology represents STAT status through `Order.priority`, `Order.is_stat`, and `StatOrder` without declaring which one is authoritative or how they must stay consistent.
  - issue stance agreement: 5/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, logic, semantics, evolution
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: structure

  - accepted lenses: semantics
  - remaining disagreement: 0/1 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none

  - issue statement: STAT order meaning can conflict across priority, is_stat, and StatOrder representations. Source finding context: Order.priority / Order.is_stat / StatOrder Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:16-24 Source finding context: STAT 주문 의미가 enum 값, boolean, 하위 타입으로 세 번 표현되어 동일 개념의 권위가 갈라진다. Source finding context: STAT은 주문의 우선순위/긴급도 의미인데, 이를 값·플래그·타입으로 동시에 두면 동일 주문이 `priority=stat`, `is_stat=false`, 비-`StatOrder`처럼 모순된 상태를 가질 수 있습니다. 개념 권위 문서가 이런 중복 표현을 허용하면 EMR/LIS 간 STAT 처리, 우선순위 큐, 알림 조건의 의미가 흔들립니다. Source finding context: STAT 표현을 하나의 원천으로 통합하세요. 예를 들어 `priority` enum만 권위로 두고 `is_stat`와 `StatOrder`를 제거하거나 파생 alias로 명시합니다. `StatOrder`를 유지한다면 `priority=stat` 및 `is_stat=true`와의 불변조건을 문서화하세요. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002 Source finding context: 주문 우선순위와 STAT 주문의 EMR/LIS 공통 의미 정의 Source finding context: 통합 시스템이나 규칙이 STAT 여부를 서로 다른 필드 또는 subtype에서 읽을 때 Source finding context: 동일 STAT 개념의 해석이 갈라져 긴급 검사 라우팅, SLA/TAT 계산, 알림 정책이 서로 다른 기준으로 작동할 수 있다. Source finding context: STAT을 Order의 속성값, boolean 파생값, subtype으로 동시에 모델링했다. Source finding context: `priority` enum에 `stat` 값이 포함되어 있다. Source finding context: `is_stat`도 STAT 주문 여부라고 설명한다. Source finding context: `StatOrder` 역시 응급(STAT) 검사 주문인 `Order` 하위 타입으로 정의된다. Source finding context: 세 표현 간 동치성, 우선순위, 불일치 처리 규칙이 없다.
  - affected purpose: Shared EMR/LIS meaning for order priority and STAT orders. Source finding context: 주문 우선순위와 STAT 주문의 EMR/LIS 공통 의미 정의
  - failure condition: Integrated systems or rules read STAT status from different fields or subtype membership. Source finding context: 통합 시스템이나 규칙이 STAT 여부를 서로 다른 필드 또는 subtype에서 읽을 때
  - impact: Urgent test routing, SLA/TAT calculation, and notification policy may operate on different STAT criteria. Source finding context: 동일 STAT 개념의 해석이 갈라져 긴급 검사 라우팅, SLA/TAT 계산, 알림 정책이 서로 다른 기준으로 작동할 수 있다.
  - root hypothesis: STAT is modeled simultaneously as an Order priority value, boolean flag, and subtype without an authority or invariant rule.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:16-24`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-014`, `finding-014.cause-001`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:16`, `finding-014.cause-002`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:17`, `finding-014.cause-003`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:20-24`, `finding-014.cause-004`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-012`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.structure`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: semantics
  - action candidates: follow_up
  - materiality: The declared purpose is shared EMR/LIS meaning for order priority and STAT orders. If integrated systems read different STAT indicators, urgent routing, SLA/TAT calculations, and notification rules can diverge even for the same order, weakening predictable urgent-order handling.
  - root cause: The starting point is that one STAT concept is modeled three ways: as a priority enum value, as a boolean flag, and as a subtype. Because no authority, derivation rule, or invariant is defined among them, the model permits contradictory states instead of one shared meaning.
  - causal path: The causal path shows `priority` includes `stat`, `is_stat` also describes STAT order status, and `StatOrder` is separately defined as a STAT `Order` subtype. With no equivalence, precedence, or conflict-handling rule across those representations, an order can be interpreted as STAT by one consumer and non-STAT by another.
  - action: Choose one authoritative STAT representation, preferably the priority value if STAT is intended as order urgency, and make any retained boolean or subtype an explicit derived alias. If `StatOrder` remains, document invariants tying it to `priority=stat` and `is_stat=true`, plus how inconsistencies are rejected or resolved.

- issue-013 (medium): The `Specimen produces Result` relation is a material semantic defect because it describes specimen material as if it actively produces the lab result, instead of serving as the source material for a test, assay, or analysis event that generates the result.
  - issue stance agreement: 4/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, semantics, coverage, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: evolution, logic
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 1/1 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: semantics
  - remaining disagreement: 0/1 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: semantics
  - issue statement: Specimen produces Result misstates specimen semantics as if the specimen were the result-producing actor. Source finding context: Specimen-to-Result 관계 의미 Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:113-114 Source finding context: `Specimen produces Result`는 검체의 의미를 결과 생산 주체로 잘못 표현한다. Source finding context: `produces`는 Order가 Report를 산출하는 관계에도 쓰입니다. 같은 동사를 검체에 적용하면 검체, 검사 수행, 분석기/assay, 결과 생성의 의미 역할이 섞입니다. EMR/LIS 통합 권위 문서에서는 검체 추적과 결과 생성 주체가 구분되어야 하므로 이 관계명은 운영 해석을 흐립니다. Source finding context: `Specimen -> Result produces`를 제거하거나 `source_for`, `used_for`, `has_result`처럼 검체의 원천 역할을 드러내는 관계로 바꾸세요. 결과 생성 주체는 `Test`/`Assay` 수행 또는 분석 이벤트 개념에 연결하는 편이 의미적으로 더 정확합니다. Source finding context: 결과의 원천인 검체에 결과 산출 행위 동사 `produces`를 부여했다. Source finding context: `Result`는 Test 수행 결과 값으로 정의된다. Source finding context: `Assay`는 분석기에서 수행되는 검사 항목으로 정의된다. Source finding context: 그런데 관계는 `Specimen -> Result produces`와 '검체가 결과를 생산한다'는 주석을 둔다. Source finding context: 같은 `produces` 관계가 `Order -> Report`에도 쓰여 산출 주체 의미를 갖는다.
  - affected purpose: Concept authority for specimen traceability and result production relationships. Source finding context: 검체 추적과 결과 산출 관계의 개념 권위
  - failure condition: Designers rely on the Specimen produces Result relation for lineage, analysis actor, or result provenance semantics. Source finding context: 검체-결과 lineage, 분석 수행 주체, 결과 생성 이벤트를 이 관계명에 의존해 설계할 때
  - impact: Specimen source, assay/test execution, analyzer action, and result provenance boundaries become ambiguous. Source finding context: 검체가 행위 주체처럼 모델링되어 assay/test 수행, 장비 분석, 결과 provenance의 의미 경계가 흐려진다.
  - root hypothesis: The model assigns the result-producing action verb to Specimen, conflating source material lineage with assay/result generation.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-003`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:49-58`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:111-114`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-015`, `finding-015.cause-001`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:57-58`, `finding-015.cause-002`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:49-55`, `finding-015.cause-003`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:113-114`, `finding-015.cause-004`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:111`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-013`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.evolution`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-016`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.structure`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: semantics
  - action candidates: follow_up
  - materiality: This weakens concept authority for specimen traceability and result-production relationships. If designers rely on this relation for lineage or provenance, the model blurs the boundary between specimen source, assay/test execution, analyzer action, and result provenance, which can lead to incorrect EMR/LIS integration interpretation.
  - root cause: The starting point is the assignment of the action verb `produces` to `Specimen`. A specimen is the material source for testing, while result production belongs to test or assay execution, or to an explicit analysis event; using an actor-like production verb on the specimen conflates those roles.
  - causal path: The causal path shows that `Result` is defined as a test execution output and `Assay` is defined as a test item performed on an analyzer, but the relation then states `Specimen -> Result produces` with a comment that the specimen produces the result. Because the same `produces` relation is also used for `Order -> Report`, where it carries an output-producing meaning, applying it to `Specimen` imports the wrong agency semantics into specimen-result lineage.
  - action: Replace or rename the relation to express specimen lineage rather than result-generation agency, using a relation such as `source_for`, `used_for`, or `has_result`. Result generation semantics should instead be connected to `Test`, `Assay`, or an explicit analysis event, so provenance and traversal paths distinguish source material from execution and production.

- issue-014 (medium): Assay is a material structural gap: it is modeled as a separate analyzer-performed execution/catalog concept, but it is not connected to ordered Tests or produced Results in the workflow graph.

Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml entities.Assay Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: entities.Assay defines department ref to Department and specimen_kind, but no relation includes Assay; relations list connects Order, Specimen, Test, Result, Report, Department, Staff only. Source finding context: Assay is structurally orphaned from the workflow graph. Source finding context: From the structure lens, an entity intended to represent the performed laboratory unit should be reachable from the order-to-report graph. In the current relation set, Test connects to Result, Result connects to Report and Specimen, and Department connects to Staff, while Assay is only connected through an attribute reference to Department. That leaves no structural path that says which catalog Test maps to which analyzer Assay or which Result came from an Assay. As a concept-authority document for EMR/LIS integration, this weakens the model because a key LIS-side execution concept cannot be traversed from ordered tests or produced results. Source finding context: Add explicit relations such as Test maps_to/realized_by Assay and Result produced_by Assay, or merge Assay into Test if they are not meant to be separate authority concepts yet. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-001 Source finding context: Use the ontology as the concept authority for EMR/LIS integration from Order through Report. Source finding context: When implementers need to map ordered catalog tests to LIS/analyzer execution units or trace a result back to the performed assay, the graph has no declared Test-Assay or Result-Assay connection. Source finding context: The integration contract becomes structurally incomplete for a central cross-system mapping, so different teams may create incompatible local joins outside the authority document. Source finding context: Assay was introduced as a separate execution/catalog concept without a declared structural relation to the existing order-result-report graph. Source finding context: Assay is defined as an analyzer-performed test item and separate from Test. Source finding context: The relations section does not include Assay as either a from or to endpoint. Source finding context: Therefore Assay cannot be traversed from Order, Test, Specimen, Result, or Report except indirectly sharing Department references.

Source finding context: The integration contract becomes structurally incomplete for a central cross-system mapping, so different teams may create incompatible local joins outside the authority document.
  - root hypothesis: Assay was introduced as a separate execution/catalog concept without structural relations to the order-result-report graph.

  - source lenses: structure

  - materiality: This weakens the ontology's declared purpose as the concept authority for EMR/LIS integration from Order through Report because implementers cannot use the authority graph to map ordered catalog tests to LIS/analyzer execution units or trace a result back to the assay that produced it. That missing bridge leaves a central cross-system mapping outside the model and encourages incompatible local joins.
  - root cause: The starting point is that Assay was introduced as its own execution/catalog concept without structural relations to the existing order-result-report graph. Because Assay is separate from Test, the model needs an explicit authority path that defines how ordered tests and results relate to that execution concept.
  - causal path: The causal chain is straightforward: Assay is defined separately from Test as an analyzer-performed item; the relations list never uses Assay as a from or to endpoint; therefore Assay cannot be traversed from Order, Test, Specimen, Result, or Report except through shared Department references. That makes the performed laboratory unit structurally orphaned from the workflow it is supposed to support.
  - action: Add explicit Test-to-Assay and Result-to-Assay relations, such as Test maps_to or realized_by Assay and Result produced_by Assay. If Assay is not intended to be a separate authority concept yet, merge it into Test instead. The ordering dependency is to resolve the Test-Assay catalog/execution bridge before treating the ontology as complete for EMR/LIS integration.

- issue-015 (medium): Order completion is materially under-specified because the ontology says an Order is completed when all Results are final, but it does not declare which Results belong to that Order for this rule.

  - issue statement: Order completion depends on all Results but the model does not structurally scope which Results belong to an Order. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml state_rules Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: Order lifecycle includes completed; Result.status values include preliminary/final/corrected; relations include Test->Result, Result->Report, Report->Order, but no Order->Result relation; state_rules says Order completes when all Result are final. Source finding context: The Order completion rule depends on Results that are not structurally scoped to an Order. Source finding context: A lifecycle rule that quantifies over 'all Result' needs a structural boundary for the result set. The current graph has Order to Report and Report to Result, plus Order to Specimen and Specimen to Result, but neither path is declared as the authoritative result scope for Order completion. In a concept authority document, that makes the completion transition structurally under-specified: different consumers could choose Report.contains Result, Specimen.produces Result, or Test.has_many Result as the source set. Source finding context: Declare the authoritative scoping relation for order completion, for example Order has_many Result via Report.contains or Specimen.produces, and update the state rule to reference that relation explicitly. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-003 Source finding context: Use the ontology as an authority for state behavior in the Order-to-Report workflow. Source finding context: When computing or implementing Order.completed, consumers must determine the set of Results for an Order without an explicit structural scope. Source finding context: Completion behavior may diverge across EMR/LIS components because the state rule depends on an unstated traversal choice. Source finding context: The lifecycle rule uses an aggregate over Result without declaring the result-set ownership path from Order. Source finding context: Order.completed is defined by all Result statuses being final. Source finding context: The relation graph has multiple indirect paths involving Order and Result but no explicit Order-to-Result scoping relation. Source finding context: Therefore the completion rule lacks a structurally unambiguous set of Results to evaluate.
  - affected purpose: Use the ontology as an authority for state behavior in the Order-to-Report workflow.
  - failure condition: Consumers must compute Order.completed and choose among indirect paths such as Report.contains Result, Specimen.produces Result, or Test.has_many Result. Source finding context: When computing or implementing Order.completed, consumers must determine the set of Results for an Order without an explicit structural scope.
  - impact: Completion behavior may diverge across EMR/LIS components because the state rule depends on an unstated traversal choice.
  - root hypothesis: The lifecycle rule aggregates over Result without declaring the authoritative result-set ownership path from Order.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-003`, `Embedded Materialized Input: entities.Order.lifecycle`, `Embedded Materialized Input: entities.Result.attributes.status`, `Embedded Materialized Input: relations list`, `Embedded Materialized Input: state_rules first item`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-019`, `finding-019.cause-001`, `finding-019.cause-002`, `Embedded Materialized Input: relations Order->Specimen, Specimen->Result, Order->Report, Report->Result, Test->Result`, `finding-019.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-015`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.evolution`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-010`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-002`, `rel-010`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: structure
  - action candidates: follow_up
  - materiality: This weakens the ontology's purpose as an authority for Order-to-Report state behavior. Consumers implementing Order.completed must choose their own traversal, such as Report.contains Result, Specimen.produces Result, or Test.has_many Result, so EMR and LIS components can compute completion differently while each appears consistent with part of the graph.
  - root cause: The root cause is the lifecycle rule's aggregate over Result without an authoritative Order-to-Result result-set ownership path. That missing scope is the starting point because the completion predicate cannot be evaluated consistently until the model defines the bounded set of Results to which all final applies.
  - causal path: The causal path is direct: Order.completed is defined by all Result statuses being final; the relation graph offers multiple indirect Order-to-Result paths and no explicit Order-to-Result scoping relation; therefore the rule lacks a structurally unambiguous result set to evaluate. Structure, logic, axiology, and coverage support this issue, while semantics and evolution narrow it to the need for a stable, declared result-set meaning and ownership path as the workflow evolves.
  - action: Declare the authoritative scoping relation for order completion and update the state rule to reference it explicitly. The action should identify whether Order's completion Results are scoped through Report.contains, Specimen.produces, another explicit Order has_many Result relation, or a defined derived path, so downstream consumers evaluate the same result set before marking an Order completed.

### Conditional Consensus
- issue-001 (no-deliberation-needed): Corrected LIS results can leave the clinician-trusted Report.result_status stale as finalized until nightly synchronization, so the ontology is not yet reliable as an EMR/LIS authority for corrected clinical information.
- issue-004 (no-deliberation-needed): The ontology must not treat critical-value notification as only a boolean. It needs an evidence-bearing notification event and an explicit workflow path from the actual result/report context to the critical-value condition and notification handling.
- issue-007 (no-deliberation-needed): Duplicated Result.status and Report.result_status vocabularies are a material high-severity issue because the ontology presents overlapping result status facts without a single authority, projection, freshness, synchronization, or evolution model.
- issue-008 (no-deliberation-needed): New Test and Assay entries currently require separate registration even though the ontology does not define an authoritative Test-to-Assay mapping or single registration authority. This is a high-severity root-cause issue because the order catalog and execution catalog can diverge as the catalog evolves.
- issue-010 (no-deliberation-needed): Order.completed is materially inconsistent because the ontology allows a completed Order to remain completed after a later Result correction changes Result.status from final to corrected, making the stated completion predicate false.
- issue-003 (no-deliberation-needed): TAT should be treated as a material authority gap: the ontology names a cross-system turnaround-time metric, but its executable calculation remains informally described and externally owned, so dashboard, EMR, and LIS consumers can drift apart.
- issue-005 (no-deliberation-needed): Specimen lifecycle coverage is materially incomplete because the shared ontology stops at analyzed and does not define post-analysis storage, retention, disposal, retrieval, or exception states.
- issue-006 (no-deliberation-needed): Catalog, threshold, unit/reference, and TAT rule facts are time-dependent but modeled as current-state concepts, so historical lab results and operational metrics cannot be reliably reconstructed under the definitions that were in force when the events occurred.
- issue-009 (no-deliberation-needed): Specimen compatibility vocabulary can diverge because Specimen and Test use closed specimen-kind enums while Assay stores specimen kind as a free string. This should be carried forward as a material medium-severity extensibility issue.
- issue-011 (no-deliberation-needed): Result.status and Report.result_status are presented as maintaining the same status information, but the ontology defines separate enum domains and only specifies the corrected -> amended synchronization case. This leaves the overall equivalence and authority relationship under-specified.
- issue-012 (no-deliberation-needed): STAT order meaning is materially ambiguous because the ontology represents STAT status through `Order.priority`, `Order.is_stat`, and `StatOrder` without declaring which one is authoritative or how they must stay consistent.
- issue-013 (no-deliberation-needed): The `Specimen produces Result` relation is a material semantic defect because it describes specimen material as if it actively produces the lab result, instead of serving as the source material for a test, assay, or analysis event that generates the result.
- issue-014 (no-deliberation-needed): Assay is a material structural gap: it is modeled as a separate analyzer-performed execution/catalog concept, but it is not connected to ordered Tests or produced Results in the workflow graph.
- issue-015 (no-deliberation-needed): Order completion is materially under-specified because the ontology says an Order is completed when all Results are final, but it does not declare which Results belong to that Order for this rule.

### Disagreement
- none

### Axiology-Proposed Additional Perspectives
- issue-001 (high): Corrected LIS results can leave the clinician-trusted Report.result_status stale as finalized until nightly synchronization, so the ontology is not yet reliable as an EMR/LIS authority for corrected clinical information.
- issue-002 (high): Critical-value notification semantics are materially weakened because `CriticalValue` carries three different meanings at once: threshold/range policy, actual patient critical-result occurrence, and notification completion state.
- issue-003 (medium): TAT should be treated as a material authority gap: the ontology names a cross-system turnaround-time metric, but its executable calculation remains informally described and externally owned, so dashboard, EMR, and LIS consumers can drift apart.

### Boundary Notes
- Structure reported insufficient structure-specific evidence, but did not dispute the workflow and status-authority issue.
- The bounded evidence does not include the external phone-log schema or an integration contract that would restore notification evidence authority.
- The bounded evidence does not include the dashboard formula, so this synthesis preserves the issue as authority-drift risk rather than a proven numerical mismatch.

Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / CriticalValue notification model Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:100-106; .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-7 Source finding context: Critical-value handling is reduced to a boolean while the notification evidence is externalized, misaligning the model with its operational-risk purpose. Source finding context: For an EMR/LIS integration authority document, the operationally important commitment is not merely whether notification happened but whether it happened immediately, to whom, and with traceable evidence. The current model treats that evidence as outside the authority boundary while still modeling the boolean, creating an under-justified tradeoff against auditability and handoff reliability. Source finding context: Model critical-value notification as an explicit entity or relation with timestamp, recipient, notifier, channel, acknowledgement, and status; if a phone log remains authoritative, add a named relation and synchronization/ownership rule instead of a standalone boolean.
