Source finding context: CriticalValue 엔티티 Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:95-101 Source finding context: `CriticalValue`라는 이름이 실제 모델인 위험 임계 범위 및 통보 상태와 의미적으로 맞지 않는다. Source finding context: 개별 환자 결과의 critical value와 검사별 임계 범위는 서로 다른 개념입니다. 여기에 `notified`까지 붙이면 임계 규칙, 발생한 위험 결과, 통보 이벤트가 한 엔티티 안에서 같은 의미처럼 보입니다. 전화 기록 대장에 통보 시각과 수신자가 있다는 주석도 통보가 별도 이벤트임을 시사합니다. Source finding context: 검사별 임계 범위는 `CriticalValueRange` 또는 `CriticalResultThreshold`로 명명하고, 실제 위험 결과는 `Result`를 참조하는 별도 `CriticalResult`/`CriticalAlert`로 분리하세요. `notified`는 통보 이벤트나 통보 기록 개념의 상태로 옮기고 수신자·시각 권위 위치를 명시하세요. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-004 Source finding context: 위험 결과 판정 및 통보 워크플로의 개념 권위 Source finding context: EMR/LIS가 이 엔티티를 critical result 인스턴스, threshold 설정, 통보 완료 상태 중 하나로 해석해야 할 때 Source finding context: 위험 결과 알림의 대상과 규칙, 통보 상태가 섞여 환자별 critical result 추적과 통보 감사 의미가 불명확해진다. Source finding context: 임계 범위, 실제 위험 결과, 통보 상태를 `CriticalValue` 하나에 합쳤다. Source finding context: `CriticalValue`의 정의는 값 자체가 아니라 위험 결과 값 범위라고 한다. Source finding context: 속성은 검사 참조와 하한/상한으로 임계 범위를 표현한다. Source finding context: 같은 엔티티에 `notified` boolean을 두고 통보 시각과 수신자는 별도 전화 기록 대장에 있다고 설명한다. Source finding context: 따라서 이름은 개별 critical value처럼 보이지만 실제 모델은 threshold와 notification state의 결합이다.
  - failure condition: A consuming system must interpret CriticalValue as threshold setting, patient critical result, or notification completion state, while only notified=true and external phone-log evidence are modeled. Source finding context: A consuming system sees `notified=true` without authoritative recipient/time/acknowledgement data or a governed link to the phone log. Source finding context: EMR/LIS가 이 엔티티를 critical result 인스턴스, threshold 설정, 통보 완료 상태 중 하나로 해석해야 할 때
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Separate the model into at least three concepts or relations: critical threshold/range policy, actual critical result or alert tied to a patient result, and notification event/record. Move `notified` onto the notification concept or derive it from governed notification evidence, and either own timestamp, recipient, notifier, channel, acknowledgement, and status in the ontology or define an explicit authoritative relation and synchronization rule to the phone ledger. This must be fixed before the next integration stage because it is a root-level safety and governance blocker.
  - remediation: Separate the model into at least three concepts or relations: critical threshold/range policy, actual critical result or alert tied to a patient result, and notification event/record. Move `notified` onto the notification concept or derive it from governed notification evidence, and either own timestamp, recipient, notifier, channel, acknowledgement, and status in the ontology or define an explicit authoritative relation and synchronization rule to the phone ledger. This must be fixed before the next integration stage because it is a root-level safety and governance blocker.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
- issue-004 (high)
  - issue: The ontology must not treat critical-value notification as only a boolean. It needs an evidence-bearing notification event and an explicit workflow path from the actual result/report context to the critical-value condition and notification handling.
  - target: The ontology lacks an evidence-bearing notification concept and a workflow relation path for critical values. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / CriticalValue notification model Source finding context: CriticalValue.attributes.notified note: "통보 완료 여부. 통보 시각과 수신자는 전화 기록 대장에 있다." Source finding context: Critical-value notification is modeled only as a boolean and omits the notification evidence concept needed for EMR/LIS authority. Source finding context: Critical-value notification is a controlled clinical action. A boolean cannot reconstruct who notified whom, when, on what basis, and whether the notice was acknowledged. For a concept authority document used in EMR/LIS integration, leaving that evidence concept outside the model weakens downstream auditability and patient-safety workflows. Source finding context: Add a CriticalValueNotification or NotificationEvent entity related to CriticalValue/Result/Order, with notifier, recipient, notified_at, channel, acknowledgement status/time, escalation fields, and source-system authority. Replace or derive notified from that event instead of making it the only modeled fact. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-001 Source finding context: Use as the conceptual authority document for EMR/LIS integration from order through report, including operational risk concepts. Source finding context: When a critical result must be communicated or audited, the ontology can only represent a completed/not-completed flag while the actor, time, recipient, and acknowledgement evidence are outside the modeled concept space. Source finding context: Implementers may integrate only a lossy boolean status, making critical-result communication non-reconstructable and weakening clinical audit, escalation, and dispute handling. Source finding context: The ontology treats critical-value notification as an attribute of the threshold concept instead of modeling the controlled notification action as its own evidence-bearing event. Source finding context: CriticalValue contains threshold fields and a boolean notified flag, but no notification event or relation to Staff/clinician/recipient. Source finding context: The only mentioned notification details, time and recipient, are described as existing in a separate phone log outside the ontology. Source finding context: Because the evidence-bearing action is outside the concept model, the ontology cannot serve as the integration authority for critical-value notification audit semantics. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml entities.CriticalValue Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: entities.CriticalValue has test_ref to Test and notified boolean, but no relation connects CriticalValue to Result, Report, Staff, Order, or notification records; relations list omits CriticalValue. Source finding context: CriticalValue is structurally disconnected from the result/report workflow. Source finding context: The structure lens checks whether necessary connections exist among declared elements. CriticalValue is not just a static catalog annotation in its definition; it includes a workflow state, notified, and says notification evidence exists in another log. Without any relation to Result or Report, the model cannot express which actual result breached a critical range or how that condition enters the order-to-report workflow. This is operationally material because critical notification is a high-risk exception path in EMR/LIS integration. Source finding context: Connect CriticalValue to the actual workflow with relations such as Result triggers CriticalValue, CriticalValue applies_to Test, CriticalValue notified_by Staff, and optionally CriticalValue documented_in NotificationRecord if notification records are in scope; otherwise remove workflow state like notified from this ontology and keep it as a pure reference range. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-002 Source finding context: Use the ontology as a concept authority for operational EMR/LIS workflow integration. Source finding context: When a result falls in a critical range and requires immediate communication, the ontology has no structural path from the Result/Report workflow to CriticalValue or notification handling. Source finding context: A critical exception process can be implemented outside the authority graph, undermining traceability and consistent integration behavior for urgent clinical notifications. Source finding context: CriticalValue was defined with workflow-relevant notification state but not attached to the workflow relation graph. Source finding context: CriticalValue represents immediately notifiable dangerous result ranges and includes notified state. Source finding context: The relation graph omits CriticalValue entirely. Source finding context: Therefore the model lacks a declared path from actual Result/Report instances to the critical value notification condition.
  - failure condition: A critical result must be communicated or audited, but the ontology can only represent a notified boolean and has no structural path from Result/Report to CriticalValue or notification handling. Source finding context: When a critical result must be communicated or audited, the ontology can only represent a completed/not-completed flag while the actor, time, recipient, and acknowledgement evidence are outside the modeled concept space. Source finding context: When a result falls in a critical range and requires immediate communication, the ontology has no structural path from the Result/Report workflow to CriticalValue or notification handling.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Add a CriticalValueNotification or NotificationEvent concept and connect it to the relevant workflow objects: CriticalValue or the triggered alert, Result, Report or Order as appropriate, Staff or other notifier, recipient, acknowledgement, escalation, channel, notification time, and the authoritative notification record/source system. The notified flag should be derived from that event or replaced by it, because the evidence-bearing action must become the authority for notification completion before downstream EMR/LIS integrations rely on the model.
  - remediation: Add a CriticalValueNotification or NotificationEvent concept and connect it to the relevant workflow objects: CriticalValue or the triggered alert, Result, Report or Order as appropriate, Staff or other notifier, recipient, acknowledgement, escalation, channel, notification time, and the authoritative notification record/source system. The notified flag should be derived from that event or replaced by it, because the evidence-bearing action must become the authority for notification completion before downstream EMR/LIS integrations rely on the model.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
- issue-007 (high)
  - issue: Duplicated Result.status and Report.result_status vocabularies are a material high-severity issue because the ontology presents overlapping result status facts without a single authority, projection, freshness, synchronization, or evolution model.
  - target: Duplicated result/report status vocabularies lack a single authority, projection, and status-evolution model. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / Result.status and Report.result_status authority coverage Source finding context: Result.status note: "LIS가 기록하며, Report.result_status에도 동일 정보가 유지된다."; Report.result_status note: "보고서에 표시되는 결과 상태의 권위 값. 임상의는 이 값을 신뢰한다." Source finding context: Duplicated result-status concepts lack a single source-of-truth coverage model across LIS and report-facing values. Source finding context: The ontology explicitly describes the same status information being maintained in two places and synchronized nightly after corrections. Without a single authority concept and a modeled projection/update event, EMR and LIS implementers lack a complete concept for status precedence, staleness, and reconciliation. Source finding context: Introduce a canonical ResultStatus concept or designate one existing field as authoritative, then model Report.result_status as a derived projection with mapping, freshness, last_synced_at, and correction/amendment event semantics. Include who/what may update the canonical status. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-004 Source finding context: EMR/LIS integration concept authority with clear precedence and operationally safe status interpretation. Source finding context: During the interval between Result.status correction and the nightly report-status sync, the ontology contains two status carriers but no canonical authority/staleness concept that consumers can use consistently. Source finding context: Clinicians or integrated systems may trust a report-facing status that is stale relative to LIS correction state, or implementations may choose different precedence rules. Source finding context: The ontology duplicates a status fact across Result and Report without covering the single authority/projection model needed for multi-system integration. Source finding context: Result.status is recorded by LIS and includes preliminary, final, corrected. Source finding context: Report.result_status contains corresponding but different values and is described as the authority value clinicians trust. Source finding context: The synchronization rule updates Report.result_status to amended only through a nightly batch after Result.status becomes corrected. Source finding context: The model lacks an explicit canonical status source, staleness marker, or synchronization event to cover this duplicated multi-system value. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml: Result.status and Report.result_status Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:64-78; clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:116-118 Source finding context: 결과 상태가 Result와 Report에 서로 다른 enum으로 중복되어 상태 확장이나 LIS 상태 변경 시 매핑 수정 없이는 연속성이 깨진다. Source finding context: 상태 모델은 운영 중 외부 LIS 상태, 보고서 수정 정책, 임상 표시 정책 변경의 영향을 가장 많이 받는 축이다. 현재는 같은 상태 의미가 두 enum에 다른 이름으로 존재하고, 동기화 규칙이 별도 문장으로만 표현된다. 새 상태를 추가하거나 상태 이름을 바꾸면 두 enum과 동기화 규칙을 함께 수정해야 하며, 야간 동기화 전에는 권위 상태가 서로 갈라질 수 있다. Source finding context: 단일 Result/Report status vocabulary 또는 명시적 StatusMapping 엔티티를 도입한다. 각 상태에 source_system, clinical_display_value, terminal 여부, transition rule을 속성으로 두어 새 상태 추가가 enum 수정과 임시 문장 규칙이 아니라 카탈로그 확장으로 처리되게 한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-003 Source finding context: EMR/LIS 통합에서 결과 상태의 권위와 상태 변경 연속성을 제공하는 목적. Source finding context: LIS가 새 결과 상태를 제공하거나 보고 정책이 바뀌어 Result.status와 Report.result_status의 enum 및 동기화 규칙을 함께 갱신해야 하는 경우. Source finding context: 상태 확장이 모델 변경과 수동 매핑 변경을 요구하고, 보고서가 임상의 신뢰 값이라고 선언된 상태에서 LIS 권위 상태와 표시 상태의 일시적 또는 지속적 불일치가 발생할 수 있다. Source finding context: 결과 상태 권위가 Result.status와 Report.result_status로 분산되어 있고 두 상태 체계의 매핑이 구조화된 개념이 아니라 문장 규칙으로만 존재한다. Source finding context: Result.status는 LIS가 기록하는 상태로 preliminary/final/corrected enum을 사용한다. Source finding context: Report.result_status는 임상의가 신뢰하는 권위 값으로 prelim/finalized/amended enum을 별도로 사용한다. Source finding context: corrected에서 amended로의 연결은 야간 1회 동기화 문장 규칙에 의존한다. Source finding context: 따라서 상태 체계 변경 시 두 enum과 문장 규칙을 함께 바꾸어야 하며, 변경 전후 데이터의 상태 연속성을 구조적으로 보장하지 못한다. Source finding context: Result.status / Report.result_status 상태 의미 모델 Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:64-78, clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:117-118 Source finding context: 동일 결과 상태를 두 권위 값으로 중복 정의하면서 enum 어휘가 서로 맞지 않는다. Source finding context: 이 온톨로지는 EMR/LIS 연동의 개념 권위 문서로 쓰일 예정인데, 결과 상태와 보고 상태가 서로 다른 이름으로 같은 정보를 주장하면 어느 값이 원천 권위인지와 상태 변환 의미가 흐려집니다. 특히 state rule은 `Result.status=corrected`일 때 `Report.result_status=amended`가 되어야 한다고만 말하므로, corrected result와 amended report가 같은 상태인지, 보고서 수정 상태인지, 결과 정정 상태인지가 섞입니다. Source finding context: 상태 개념을 하나의 canonical enum으로 정하거나, `ResultStatus`와 `ReportStatus`를 분리하되 명시적인 mapping 표를 둡니다. 각 상태의 의미, 원천 권위, 전이 조건을 같은 어휘로 맞추고 `prelim/preliminary`, `final/finalized`, `corrected/amended`를 alias인지 별도 상태인지 선언하세요. Source finding context: `Report.result_status` 설명은 보고서에 표시되는 결과 상태의 권위 값이라고 한다. Source finding context: 두 필드의 enum 값은 서로 다른 어휘를 사용한다.   - failure condition: Consumers must interpret or extend Result.status and Report.result_status across LIS and clinician-facing paths while the ontology uses separate enum names and only partial synchronization rules. Source finding context: During the interval between Result.status correction and the nightly report-status sync, the ontology contains two status carriers but no canonical authority/staleness concept that consumers can use consistently.   - rationale: Fix this before the next stage by introducing either a canonical ResultStatus concept or an explicit StatusMapping/projection model.   - remediation: Fix this before the next stage by introducing either a canonical ResultStatus concept or an explicit StatusMapping/projection model. The model should define source authority, report-facing projection, clinical display value, alias or distinct-state semantics, freshness or last_synced_at, synchronization/correction events, update authority, terminal status, and transition rules so future LIS states or reporting-policy changes extend the catalog rather than requiring coordinated enum and prose-rule edits.

- issue-008 (high)
  - issue: New Test and Assay entries currently require separate registration even though the ontology does not define an authoritative Test-to-Assay mapping or single registration authority. This is a high-severity root-cause issue because the order catalog and execution catalog can diverge as the catalog evolves.
  - target: New tests and assays require dual registration without an authoritative Test-to-Assay mapping. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml: Test/Assay catalog model Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:41-55; clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:121-122 Source finding context: 신규 검사 항목 추가 시 Test와 Assay를 이중 등록해야 하며, 둘 사이의 권위 관계나 매핑 관계가 없다. Source finding context: EMR/LIS 통합의 개념 권위 문서라면 주문 카탈로그(Test)와 수행 카탈로그(Assay) 사이의 연속성이 확장 가능한 구조로 표현되어야 한다. 현재 구조에서는 새 검사, 패널 분해, 장비별 수행 코드 추가 때 동일한 의미를 두 엔티티에 수동 복제해야 하므로 코드 불일치와 누락이 생기기 쉽고, 기존 구조를 수정하지 않는 확장이 어렵다. Source finding context: Test를 주문 권위 카탈로그로 두고 Assay를 수행 구현으로 명확히 분리한 뒤 Test-to-Assay 매핑 관계를 추가한다. 신규 항목 등록 권위는 한 곳으로 정하고, 장비/부서별 변형은 매핑 엔티티나 관계 속성으로 확장되게 한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-001 Source finding context: EMR/LIS 연동 설계의 개념 권위 문서로서 주문 카탈로그와 수행 카탈로그의 확장 가능한 연결을 제공하는 목적. Source finding context: 신규 검사 항목이나 장비별 Assay가 추가될 때 Test와 Assay를 각각 등록해야 하지만 둘 사이의 권위 매핑이 없어 한쪽만 갱신되거나 의미가 갈라지는 경우. Source finding context: 주문에서 수행, 수행에서 결과로 이어지는 카탈로그 연속성이 깨져 EMR/LIS 통합 설계자가 어느 코드가 권위인지 판단할 수 없고, 신규 항목 추가가 반복될수록 재정비나 마이그레이션이 필요해진다. Source finding context: 주문 카탈로그(Test)와 수행 카탈로그(Assay)를 별도 엔티티로 만들었지만, 둘 사이의 canonical mapping 또는 단일 등록 권위를 모델링하지 않았다. Source finding context: Test는 주문 가능한 카탈로그 단위로 정의되고 Assay는 분석기 수행 단위로 별도 정의된다. Source finding context: 현재 신규 항목은 두 곳 모두에 등록한다고 명시되어 수동 이중 등록이 확장 경로가 된다. Source finding context: 두 엔티티 사이의 관계 목록에는 Test-to-Assay 연결이 없어 이중 등록된 항목의 동일성이나 변형 관계를 추적할 수 없다.
  - failure condition: A new test item or analyzer-specific assay is added, but Test and Assay must be registered separately without an authoritative mapping. Source finding context: 신규 검사 항목이나 장비별 Assay가 추가될 때 Test와 Assay를 각각 등록해야 하지만 둘 사이의 권위 매핑이 없어 한쪽만 갱신되거나 의미가 갈라지는 경우.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Make Test the authoritative order catalog concept and Assay the execution implementation concept, then add an authoritative Test-to-Assay mapping or relation. New-item registration ownership should be assigned to one authority, while analyzer, department, panel, or implementation variants should be represented through the mapping entity or relation properties so extensions do not require semantic duplication.
  - remediation: Make Test the authoritative order catalog concept and Assay the execution implementation concept, then add an authoritative Test-to-Assay mapping or relation. New-item registration ownership should be assigned to one authority, while analyzer, department, panel, or implementation variants should be represented through the mapping entity or relation properties so extensions do not require semantic duplication.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
- issue-010 (high)
  - issue: Order.completed is materially inconsistent because the ontology allows a completed Order to remain completed after a later Result correction changes Result.status from final to corrected, making the stated completion predicate false.
  - target: Order.completed can remain true after a later allowed correction makes the stated completion predicate false. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml state_rules: Order completion rule and Report correction rule Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml state_rules: "Order는 모든 Result가 final일 때 completed로 전이한다." and "Report는 released_at 기록 시점에 finalized 상태가 된다. 단, Result.status가 corrected로 바뀌면 Report.result_status는 amended가 되어야 한다(동기화 배치는 야간 1회)."; Result.status values: [preliminary, final, corrected]; Order.lifecycle: [draft, placed, in_progress, completed, cancelled] Source finding context: Order completion is defined from a reversible Result.status condition while the model also allows later correction of Result.status, making the completed state inconsistent after an allowed correction event. Source finding context: The conflict is formed by explicit claims only: completion requires all related Results to be final, and correction later changes at least one Result.status to corrected, which is a distinct enum value from final. Because completed is a lifecycle state with no stated retraction or amendment transition, a permitted later status change can leave an Order simultaneously treated as completed and no longer satisfying its declared completion condition. This matters for an EMR/LIS conceptual authority document because downstream integration can derive closure from Order.completed while report/result state has moved beyond the condition that justified closure. Source finding context: Define completion against a stable milestone or versioned snapshot, or add explicit post-final states/transitions such as amended/reopened and specify how Order.completed is preserved or revised when a Result becomes corrected. If corrected should still count as final for Order completion, state that explicitly, e.g. completion condition is all Results in final-or-corrected terminal statuses. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-001 Source finding context: Use of the ontology as the conceptual authority for EMR/LIS integration state behavior from Order through Report. Source finding context: A Result that was final at Order completion is later changed to corrected under the ontology's own Report correction rule. Source finding context: Integrations can observe or propagate an Order.completed state whose stated predicate is false, weakening trust in closure, downstream billing/reporting triggers, and clinician-facing workflow state. Source finding context: The completion rule uses the mutable Result.status value final as the necessary condition for a durable Order.completed lifecycle state while the same model permits later mutation of that Result.status to corrected. Source finding context: Order completion is triggered when all Results are final. Source finding context: Result.status has corrected as a separate value from final and the model allows Result.status to change to corrected after release. Source finding context: The Order lifecycle does not include a post-completion amended/reopened state or any rule preserving completed as a snapshot despite later correction.
  - failure condition: A Result that was final at Order completion is later changed to corrected under the ontology's own correction rule. Source finding context: A Result that was final at Order completion is later changed to corrected under the ontology's own Report correction rule.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Fix this before the next stage by defining completion against a stable milestone or versioned snapshot, or by adding explicit corrected/amended/reopened semantics and terminal-state rules. If corrected Results should still satisfy completion, the ontology must state that final-or-corrected terminal statuses count for Order completion; otherwise it must define how Order.completed changes or is qualified after correction.
  - remediation: Fix this before the next stage by defining completion against a stable milestone or versioned snapshot, or by adding explicit corrected/amended/reopened semantics and terminal-state rules. If corrected Results should still satisfy completion, the ontology must state that final-or-corrected terminal statuses count for Order completion; otherwise it must define how Order.completed changes or is qualified after correction.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
- issue-003 (medium)
  - issue: TAT should be treated as a material authority gap: the ontology names a cross-system turnaround-time metric, but its executable calculation remains informally described and externally owned, so dashboard, EMR, and LIS consumers can drift apart.
  - target: TAT can drift across dashboard, EMR, and LIS consumers because its formula is described informally while owned externally. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / turnaround_time ownership Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:6-8,126-128; .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-7 Source finding context: Delegating TAT calculation to a dashboard-owned formula drifts from the ontology's concept-authority purpose. Source finding context: TAT is defined in the target as `collected_at` to `released_at`, so letting another consumer maintain its own calculation creates a hidden local optimization: dashboards can evolve a metric separately from the concept authority that integrations are supposed to share. Source finding context: Promote TAT to an authoritative derived concept or metric rule in the ontology, name the owning timestamps and edge cases, and require downstream dashboards to consume or mirror that rule with explicit versioning. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-003 Source finding context: Use the ontology as a shared conceptual authority for EMR/LIS integration. Source finding context: Dashboard, EMR, and LIS consumers compute TAT differently because the ontology defines the metric informally while ownership remains local to the dashboard team. Source finding context: A key workflow metric can diverge across systems, reducing the ontology's usefulness as a shared authority for operational interpretation. Source finding context: The ontology defines a cross-system metric but leaves its executable ownership outside the authority document. Source finding context: The target declares itself intended as the EMR/LIS integration concept authority document. Source finding context: The notes define TAT but state the dashboard team maintains its own calculation formula. Source finding context: External metric ownership enables divergent local calculations, weakening shared concept authority.
  - failure condition: Dashboard, EMR, and LIS consumers compute TAT differently because the ontology defines the metric informally while formula ownership remains local to the dashboard team. Source finding context: Dashboard, EMR, and LIS consumers compute TAT differently because the ontology defines the metric informally while ownership remains local to the dashboard team.
  - candidates: follow_up
  - rationale: Promote TAT to an authoritative derived concept or versioned metric rule in the ontology. The rule should name the owning timestamps, calculation semantics, edge cases, and downstream consumption or mirroring expectations so dashboards, EMR, and LIS integrations all depend on the same governed metric definition.
  - remediation: Promote TAT to an authoritative derived concept or versioned metric rule in the ontology. The rule should name the owning timestamps, calculation semantics, edge cases, and downstream consumption or mirroring expectations so dashboards, EMR, and LIS integrations all depend on the same governed metric definition.

- issue-005 (medium)
  - issue: Specimen lifecycle coverage is materially incomplete because the shared ontology stops at analyzed and does not define post-analysis storage, retention, disposal, retrieval, or exception states.
  - target: Specimen storage, retention, disposal, retrieval, and exception states are omitted after analyzed. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / Specimen lifecycle Source finding context: Specimen.lifecycle: [collected, received, in_analysis, analyzed]; state_rules: "Specimen이 analyzed가 된 뒤의 보관/폐기 처리는 각 부서 내규를 따른다." Source finding context: Specimen lifecycle stops at analyzed and omits post-analysis storage, retention, disposal, and exception states. Source finding context: Specimen handling does not end at analysis in operational workflows. Post-analysis storage, retention, disposal, and possible retrieval/retest are lifecycle concepts that EMR/LIS integration may need to coordinate or audit. Delegating them to local policy leaves a coverage gap in the concept authority document. Source finding context: Extend Specimen lifecycle with post-analysis states such as stored, retained, disposed, rejected, lost, or recalled as appropriate, and add retention/disposal event concepts with actor, time, reason, and department policy reference if policy variability must remain. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-002 Source finding context: A complete clinical lab workflow ontology from order to report that can guide operational EMR/LIS integration. Source finding context: After Specimen reaches analyzed, the model has no canonical state or event vocabulary for storage, retention, disposal, or post-analysis exceptions. Source finding context: Systems using the ontology may diverge on specimen end-of-life semantics, making retention, disposal audit, retesting, and exception handling inconsistent across departments. Source finding context: The ontology bounds Specimen lifecycle to the analytical phase and leaves end-of-life handling outside the shared concept model. Source finding context: Specimen lifecycle enumerates only collected, received, in_analysis, and analyzed. Source finding context: The only post-analyzed handling rule says storage/disposal follows each department's internal rules, without modeling shared states or events. Source finding context: This leaves the integration authority without canonical post-analysis lifecycle concepts for specimen end-of-life operations.
  - failure condition: After Specimen reaches analyzed, the model has no canonical state or event vocabulary for storage, retention, disposal, or post-analysis exceptions.
  - candidates: follow_up
  - rationale: Extend the Specimen lifecycle with governed post-analysis states such as stored, retained, disposed, rejected, lost, or recalled as appropriate, and add retention/disposal event concepts with actor, time, reason, and department policy reference where policy variability must remain. This should follow the existing lifecycle authority so local policies can vary without removing shared audit and integration semantics.
  - remediation: Extend the Specimen lifecycle with governed post-analysis states such as stored, retained, disposed, rejected, lost, or recalled as appropriate, and add retention/disposal event concepts with actor, time, reason, and department policy reference where policy variability must remain. This should follow the existing lifecycle authority so local policies can vary without removing shared audit and integration semantics.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
- issue-006 (medium)
  - issue: Catalog, threshold, unit/reference, and TAT rule facts are time-dependent but modeled as current-state concepts, so historical lab results and operational metrics cannot be reliably reconstructed under the definitions that were in force when the events occurred.
  - target: Catalogs, thresholds, units, and TAT rules lack effective-period and history coverage for historical reconstruction. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / Test, Assay, CriticalValue, and TAT reference data Source finding context: Test and Assay catalog attributes lack effective dates/history; CriticalValue has bounds but no effective period; notes: "현재 신규 항목은 두 곳 모두에 등록한다" and "대시보드 팀이 자체 계산식을 유지한다." Source finding context: Time-dependent catalog, threshold, and calculation concepts lack effective-period and history coverage. Source finding context: Catalog definitions, assay mappings, critical thresholds, units, and TAT formulas are time-dependent operational facts. Without validity intervals or history, a past result/report cannot be reliably interpreted under the rules and thresholds that were in force when it was produced. Source finding context: Add versioned/effective-dated concepts for Test, Assay, CriticalValue thresholds, units/reference metadata, and TAT calculation rules. Relate Result/Report to the effective catalog or rule version used at verification/release time. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-003 Source finding context: Conceptual authority for EMR/LIS integration that supports reliable historical reconstruction of lab results and operational metrics. Source finding context: When catalog mappings, thresholds, units, or TAT formulas change, the ontology has no concept for the value version that applied at a past collection, verification, or release time. Source finding context: Historical reports, corrected results, audits, and dashboard metrics may be reinterpreted using current definitions rather than the definitions in force at the event time. Source finding context: The model represents mutable reference data as unversioned current-state entities and leaves at least one derived calculation outside the authority model. Source finding context: Test and Assay are modeled as catalog/current execution units without effective dates, version, or history attributes. Source finding context: CriticalValue thresholds are modeled as bounds for a Test but without an effective period or threshold version. Source finding context: TAT is described as a value calculated by a dashboard team's own formula, with no calculation-rule concept or version in the ontology. Source finding context: Therefore past results and metrics cannot be reconstructed against the catalog, threshold, and formula state that existed at the relevant time.
  - failure condition: Catalog mappings, thresholds, units, or TAT formulas change, but the ontology has no concept for the version that applied at collection, verification, or release time. Source finding context: When catalog mappings, thresholds, units, or TAT formulas change, the ontology has no concept for the value version that applied at a past collection, verification, or release time.
  - candidates: follow_up
  - rationale: Add versioned or effective-dated concepts for Test, Assay, CriticalValue thresholds, unit/reference metadata, and TAT calculation rules, then relate Result and Report records to the effective catalog or rule version used at verification or release time. The ordering matters: first establish the versioned authority concepts, then wire historical result/report interpretation to those versions so reconstruction uses event-time rules instead of current-state defaults.
  - remediation: Add versioned or effective-dated concepts for Test, Assay, CriticalValue thresholds, unit/reference metadata, and TAT calculation rules, then relate Result and Report records to the effective catalog or rule version used at verification or release time. The ordering matters: first establish the versioned authority concepts, then wire historical result/report interpretation to those versions so reconstruction uses event-time rules instead of current-state defaults.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing

- issue-011 (medium)
  - issue: Result.status and Report.result_status are presented as maintaining the same status information, but the ontology defines separate enum domains and only specifies the corrected -> amended synchronization case. This leaves the overall equivalence and authority relationship under-specified.
  - target: Result.status and Report.result_status claim to maintain the same information but lack a total equivalence rule. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml Result.status and Report.result_status synchronization rule Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml Result.status values: [preliminary, final, corrected]; Report.result_status values: [prelim, finalized, amended]; state_rules: "Report는 released_at 기록 시점에 finalized 상태가 된다. 단, Result.status가 corrected로 바뀌면 Report.result_status는 amended가 되어야 한다(동기화 배치는 야간 1회)." Source finding context: The rule obligates Report.result_status to be amended when any Result.status becomes corrected, but the ontology also records the same status information in two non-identical enum domains, leaving same-information synchronization under-specified as a formal constraint. Source finding context: This is not merely naming preference: the ontology asserts duplicate maintenance of the same information while declaring two different enum domains. A model instance can satisfy each enum locally, but the cross-field sameness claim lacks the explicit equivalence relation needed to satisfy the synchronization obligation for all allowed states. For an authority document, that creates conflicting implementer interpretations about which field is authoritative and when mismatched statuses are valid. Source finding context: Add a total status mapping table or unify the enum. For example: preliminary <-> prelim, final <-> finalized, corrected <-> amended, plus rules for multiple Results in one Report. Also clarify whether Report.result_status is derived from Result.status or is the authority with an explicit propagation rule. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-002 Source finding context: Conceptual authority for EMR/LIS status synchronization between LIS result state and clinician-facing report state. Source finding context: Any implementation must decide whether and how Result.status and Report.result_status represent the same status information outside the single corrected -> amended case. Source finding context: Because the ontology gives two enum sets for the same maintained information without a total equivalence relation, different systems can satisfy local enum constraints while disagreeing on valid cross-system state, undermining integration precedence and invalid-input behavior. Source finding context: The ontology splits one declared status information concept into two separate enum domains and only partially constrains their equivalence. Source finding context: Result.status is declared to be maintained also in Report.result_status as the same information. Source finding context: The two fields have non-identical enum values and Report.result_status is also described as the authority value trusted by clinicians. Source finding context: Only the corrected-to-amended synchronization case is explicitly constrained, leaving other allowed states without a formal equivalence rule.
  - failure condition: An implementation must decide whether and how Result.status and Report.result_status represent the same status information outside the corrected-to-amended case. Source finding context: Any implementation must decide whether and how Result.status and Report.result_status represent the same status information outside the single corrected -> amended case.
  - candidates: follow_up
  - rationale: Add a total status mapping table or unify the enum. If two fields remain, clarify whether Report.result_status is derived from Result.status or is authoritative, define the propagation rule, and include rules for multiple Results in one Report so every allowed state has a determinate cross-field interpretation.
  - remediation: Add a total status mapping table or unify the enum. If two fields remain, clarify whether Report.result_status is derived from Result.status or is authoritative, define the propagation rule, and include rules for multiple Results in one Report so every allowed state has a determinate cross-field interpretation.

- issue-013 (medium)
  - issue: The `Specimen produces Result` relation is a material semantic defect because it describes specimen material as if it actively produces the lab result, instead of serving as the source material for a test, assay, or analysis event that generates the result.
  - target: Specimen produces Result misstates specimen semantics as if the specimen were the result-producing actor. Source finding context: Specimen-to-Result 관계 의미 Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:113-114 Source finding context: `Specimen produces Result`는 검체의 의미를 결과 생산 주체로 잘못 표현한다. Source finding context: `produces`는 Order가 Report를 산출하는 관계에도 쓰입니다. 같은 동사를 검체에 적용하면 검체, 검사 수행, 분석기/assay, 결과 생성의 의미 역할이 섞입니다. EMR/LIS 통합 권위 문서에서는 검체 추적과 결과 생성 주체가 구분되어야 하므로 이 관계명은 운영 해석을 흐립니다. Source finding context: `Specimen -> Result produces`를 제거하거나 `source_for`, `used_for`, `has_result`처럼 검체의 원천 역할을 드러내는 관계로 바꾸세요. 결과 생성 주체는 `Test`/`Assay` 수행 또는 분석 이벤트 개념에 연결하는 편이 의미적으로 더 정확합니다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-003 Source finding context: 검체 추적과 결과 산출 관계의 개념 권위 Source finding context: 검체-결과 lineage, 분석 수행 주체, 결과 생성 이벤트를 이 관계명에 의존해 설계할 때 Source finding context: 검체가 행위 주체처럼 모델링되어 assay/test 수행, 장비 분석, 결과 provenance의 의미 경계가 흐려진다. Source finding context: 결과의 원천인 검체에 결과 산출 행위 동사 `produces`를 부여했다. Source finding context: `Result`는 Test 수행 결과 값으로 정의된다. Source finding context: `Assay`는 분석기에서 수행되는 검사 항목으로 정의된다. Source finding context: 그런데 관계는 `Specimen -> Result produces`와 '검체가 결과를 생산한다'는 주석을 둔다. Source finding context: 같은 `produces` 관계가 `Order -> Report`에도 쓰여 산출 주체 의미를 갖는다.

- issue-014 (medium)
  - issue: Assay is a material structural gap: it is modeled as a separate analyzer-performed execution/catalog concept, but it is not connected to ordered Tests or produced Results in the workflow graph.
  - target: Assay is structurally orphaned from ordered tests and produced results. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml entities.Assay Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: entities.Assay defines department ref to Department and specimen_kind, but no relation includes Assay; relations list connects Order, Specimen, Test, Result, Report, Department, Staff only. Source finding context: Assay is structurally orphaned from the workflow graph. Source finding context: From the structure lens, an entity intended to represent the performed laboratory unit should be reachable from the order-to-report graph. In the current relation set, Test connects to Result, Result connects to Report and Specimen, and Department connects to Staff, while Assay is only connected through an attribute reference to Department. That leaves no structural path that says which catalog Test maps to which analyzer Assay or which Result came from an Assay. As a concept-authority document for EMR/LIS integration, this weakens the model because a key LIS-side execution concept cannot be traversed from ordered tests or produced results. Source finding context: Add explicit relations such as Test maps_to/realized_by Assay and Result produced_by Assay, or merge Assay into Test if they are not meant to be separate authority concepts yet. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-001 Source finding context: Use the ontology as the concept authority for EMR/LIS integration from Order through Report. Source finding context: When implementers need to map ordered catalog tests to LIS/analyzer execution units or trace a result back to the performed assay, the graph has no declared Test-Assay or Result-Assay connection. Source finding context: The integration contract becomes structurally incomplete for a central cross-system mapping, so different teams may create incompatible local joins outside the authority document. Source finding context: Assay was introduced as a separate execution/catalog concept without a declared structural relation to the existing order-result-report graph. Source finding context: Assay is defined as an analyzer-performed test item and separate from Test. Source finding context: The relations section does not include Assay as either a from or to endpoint. Source finding context: Therefore Assay cannot be traversed from Order, Test, Specimen, Result, or Report except indirectly sharing Department references.
  - failure condition: Implementers need to map ordered catalog tests to LIS/analyzer execution units or trace a result back to the performed assay. Source finding context: When implementers need to map ordered catalog tests to LIS/analyzer execution units or trace a result back to the performed assay, the graph has no declared Test-Assay or Result-Assay connection.
  - candidates: follow_up
  - rationale: Add explicit Test-to-Assay and Result-to-Assay relations, such as Test maps_to or realized_by Assay and Result produced_by Assay. If Assay is not intended to be a separate authority concept yet, merge it into Test instead. The ordering dependency is to resolve the Test-Assay catalog/execution bridge before treating the ontology as complete for EMR/LIS integration.
  - remediation: Add explicit Test-to-Assay and Result-to-Assay relations, such as Test maps_to or realized_by Assay and Result produced_by Assay. If Assay is not intended to be a separate authority concept yet, merge it into Test instead. The ordering dependency is to resolve the Test-Assay catalog/execution bridge before treating the ontology as complete for EMR/LIS integration.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
- issue-015 (medium)
  - issue: Order completion is materially under-specified because the ontology says an Order is completed when all Results are final, but it does not declare which Results belong to that Order for this rule.
  - target: Order completion depends on all Results but the model does not structurally scope which Results belong to an Order. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml state_rules Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: Order lifecycle includes completed; Result.status values include preliminary/final/corrected; relations include Test->Result, Result->Report, Report->Order, but no Order->Result relation; state_rules says Order completes when all Result are final. Source finding context: The Order completion rule depends on Results that are not structurally scoped to an Order. Source finding context: A lifecycle rule that quantifies over 'all Result' needs a structural boundary for the result set. The current graph has Order to Report and Report to Result, plus Order to Specimen and Specimen to Result, but neither path is declared as the authoritative result scope for Order completion. In a concept authority document, that makes the completion transition structurally under-specified: different consumers could choose Report.contains Result, Specimen.produces Result, or Test.has_many Result as the source set. Source finding context: Declare the authoritative scoping relation for order completion, for example Order has_many Result via Report.contains or Specimen.produces, and update the state rule to reference that relation explicitly. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-49a71a98/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-003 Source finding context: Use the ontology as an authority for state behavior in the Order-to-Report workflow. Source finding context: When computing or implementing Order.completed, consumers must determine the set of Results for an Order without an explicit structural scope. Source finding context: Completion behavior may diverge across EMR/LIS components because the state rule depends on an unstated traversal choice. Source finding context: The lifecycle rule uses an aggregate over Result without declaring the result-set ownership path from Order. Source finding context: Order.completed is defined by all Result statuses being final. Source finding context: The relation graph has multiple indirect paths involving Order and Result but no explicit Order-to-Result scoping relation. Source finding context: Therefore the completion rule lacks a structurally unambiguous set of Results to evaluate.
  - failure condition: Consumers must compute Order.completed and choose among indirect paths such as Report.contains Result, Specimen.produces Result, or Test.has_many Result. Source finding context: When computing or implementing Order.completed, consumers must determine the set of Results for an Order without an explicit structural scope.
  - candidates: follow_up
  - rationale: Declare the authoritative scoping relation for order completion and update the state rule to reference it explicitly. The action should identify whether Order's completion Results are scoped through Report.contains, Specimen.produces, another explicit Order has_many Result relation, or a defined derived path, so downstream consumers evaluate the same result set before marking an Order completed.
  - remediation: Declare the authoritative scoping relation for order completion and update the state rule to reference it explicitly. The action should identify whether Order's completion Results are scoped through Report.contains, Specimen.produces, another explicit Order has_many Result relation, or a defined derived path, so downstream consumers evaluate the same result set before marking an Order completed.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
